NFL trade deadline 2026: Denver Broncos latest news and rumors

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The NFL trade deadline is Tuesday, November 10th at 2 p.m. MT, and our Denver Broncos could be an active team prior to the deadline. They have been widely connected to Colts star running back Jonathan Taylor in the days leading up to the deadline, but according to 9NEWS Denver's Mike Klis, the team could explore adding a weapon or two to their offense. He mentions running back, tight end, and wide receiver as the positions that they could add.

Looking at the Broncos' needs heading into the deadline, the most glaring one is running back. Now, the emergence of rookie running back Jonah Coleman helps, but the Broncos still need more from the position. RJ Harvey and Tyler Badie are on the injured reserve, and veteran JK Dobbins has been struggling all season. So, adding another back to pair with Coleman would make sense.

The question is, will they go big and add a Jonathan Taylor or Breece Hall? Or make a smaller move for someone like Tony Pollard or someone else?

Klis mentions wide receiver, and while it may not be the most pressing need right now, it could be in a few months. Jaylen Waddle has not performed as expected thus far; Courtland Sutton will be 32 next season and has a large cap hit(Also restructured his deal and could be off the team next season); Pat Bryant has been very good, but has been on the IR; Marvin Mims is a pending free agent, while Troy Franklin is buried on the depth chart. Could the Broncos be looking to add a wide receiver who could contribute to this team this season and moving forward? This doesn't seem as likely, but I wouldn't rule it out if the right deal comes their way.

As for tight end, I don't know. The only name that could move the needle and that might be available is Dallas Goedert, but is that even worth it? He would certainly improve that room, but the Broncos really do not utilize their tight ends in the passing game. Evan Engram has had a disappointing year, and blocking specialist Nate Adkins leads the group (and, funny enough, the team) in TD receptions with 3, but he only has 6 total catches thus far.

We'll be tracking all the Broncos news and rumors throughout the weekend and right up to the deadline. It's supposed to be an active deadline, so we'll see if our Denver Broncos make any splashes or not in the coming days.

Colts trade G Quenton Nelson to the Seahawks

Colts trade DL DeForest Buckner to the 49ers

Titans trade DL Jeffrey Simmons to the Bears

Cardinals trade QB Jacoby Brissett to the Ravens

Falcons trade S Jessie Bates to the Jaguars

Saints trade DL Davon Godchaux to the Jaguars

Eagles trade DT Jordan Davis to the Jaguars

Eagles trade EDGE Arnold Ebeketie to the Bengals

Broncos may explore adding a "weapon or two" prior to the deadline, per 9NEW Denver's Mike Klis – Source

Broncos are reportedly doing everything in their power to trade for Colts RB Jonathan Taylor, per DNVR's Zac Stevens – Source

The Broncos might be a little less inclined to trade for Taylor now, according to SI's Albert Breer – Source

Veteran RB JK Dobbins name came up in trade talks, but that could be less likely now that RJ Harvey is on IR, per SI's Albert Breer – Source

Broncos have been calling around the league for tight end help, and even attempted to trade for Cardinals star TE Trey McBride, but the Cardinals were not interested – Source

NFL Insider Jordan Schultz says the Broncos are looking to add a running back or tight end prior to the trade deadline – Source

Broncos have made more inquiries on safeties and tight ends than running backs, and a trade seems unlikely as of tonight – Source

Teams have called the Denver Broncos about TE Evan Engram, but he is considered unlikely to move due to the Broncos not currently having a replacement – Source

Scientists tracked millions of phones to uncover patterns in carbon emissions

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Scientists used anonymized cell phone mobility data to estimate carbon emissions, testing the approach across four countries.

When COVID-19 lockdowns froze the planet in early 2020, carbon emissions plummeted almost overnight.

The problem? The instruments tracking those emissions couldn’t keep up.

Conventional inventories lag reality by months. Satellite inversions come with their own trade-offs between resolution and coverage.

A new study explores another approach: using people’s movements to help estimate carbon emissions.

The research was led by Mengning Wang and Xin Lu from China’s National University of Defense Technology.

Collaborators included Xiaoling Zhang from the University of Hong Kong and scholars from various international institutions.

The researchers turned anonymized cell phone signals from 318 million Chinese mobile users into what amounts to a near-real-time carbon meter.

They tested the framework across four countries.

Commuting, deliveries, business trips, and holiday travel often generate carbon emissions.

The researchers reasoned that if so many carbon-producing processes involve physically moving people and goods, then the movement itself might serve as a fast measuring stick.

They took anonymized signaling data from China Unicom and converted it into daily intercity mobility networks.

Each city became a node and each flow of people between cities became an edge.

From these networks they extracted structural features – how central a city is, how tightly clustered its connections are, how many links it maintains – and combined those with population and geographic data.

Then they trained machine learning models against the GRACED dataset, which tracks daily CO2 emissions across seven sectors: power, industry, residential consumption, ground transport, domestic aviation, international aviation, and shipping.

The window researchers studied was January and February 2020. It’s hard to imagine a harder test.

Spring Festival travel sent 68.74 million daily intercity trips surging through the network. Then lockdowns hit.

Mobility crashed by 71.3%. Multi-source carbon emissions dropped from an average of roughly 356.5 million kilograms of carbon per day to a minimum of 209.6 million – a 41.2% collapse. The whole thing happened in weeks.

And yet the correlation between daily national mobility and CO2 emissions held firm at r = 0.89. At the city level, it was a more modest but still significant r = 0.49.

The best-performing model – LightGBM, a tree-based ensemble – achieved R² values of 1.00 for multi-source emissions, 0.99 for domestic aviation, and 0.98 for ground transportation across 366 cities and 60 days.

Those are extraordinary numbers for something as messy as urban carbon output.

The real surprise came when the framework crossed borders.

Using open-source mobility datasets, the team applied the same approach to Italy, the continental United States, and Mexico.

The model reached R² values of 0.91, 0.95, and 0.99 respectively against the GRACED inventory.

Tested against a completely independent dataset – ODIAC, which derives emissions from nighttime light imagery and power-plant data at 1 km resolution – it still scored between 0.90 and 0.99.

Four countries, two independent benchmarks, consistent accuracy.

Something unexpected emerged from the mobility data. The Louvain community detection algorithm carved China’s 366 cities into 11 functional clusters.

These are groupings defined not by provincial borders but by how people actually move between them. Beijing-Tianjin-Hebei formed one cluster, pumping out 62.75 million kilograms of carbon daily.

The Yangtze River Delta formed another, at 49.71 million.

These clusters aren’t just descriptive. More central, more connected clusters emit more carbon.

But here’s the policy hook. China’s existing climate governance tends to target a handful of major emitting cities while overlooking smaller ones that sit in structurally critical positions within the network.

The mobility-defined clusters offer something different: a template for coordinated regional decarbonization that matches how economies actually function rather than how administrative maps happen to be drawn.

The authors aren’t overselling this. They call mobility a “partial but observable proxy” – a complement to inventories, not a replacement.

The Chinese analysis covers a single extraordinary two-month window. The data captures intercity movement, not what happens within a city.

Mobility alone may not fully capture emissions changes driven by seasonal energy demand, holiday schedules, or weekly cycles.

There are subtler problems too. Cellular signaling data inherits all the biases of telecom coverage – regional disparities, operator market share, sampling artifacts.

And the fundamental link between mobility and emissions could shift as remote work spreads, transport electrifies, and travel patterns reshape.

A correlation forged during the most dramatic behavioral disruption in modern history might not hold the same way during a quiet Tuesday in 2030.

Still, the core appeal is hard to argue with. Standard emissions inventories are accurate but glacially slow.

With timely access to mobility data, the framework could provide frequently updated emissions estimates.

If it scales, climate policymakers would gain a behavior-informed reading of the present rather than a months-old accounting of the past.

The team calls for fusing their mobility approach with remote sensing, energy structure data, and meteorological variables.

The ambition is to make carbon monitoring something closer to weather forecasting: imperfect, always updating, but fast enough to actually be useful.

The study is published in the journal The Innovation.

To Penny, or Not to Penny?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Few shoes are as essential in the modern menswear landscape as the classic leather loafer. Whether you’re dressing up or dressing down, it is the “the Swiss Army Knife of shoes,” an any-occasion solution to many footwear conundrums. But within the broader loafer genus, one particular species—the penny loafer—includes a divisive design feature: a small, coin-shaped slot in the leather strap “saddle” across the vamp of the shoe.

As the lore goes, Ivy Style pioneers and sock-hopping teens in the mid-20th century liked to store a penny in their shoes as “payphone change.” But now that payphones are a thing of the misty-eyed past and the American one-cent penny is no longer minted for circulation, is it still cool to put a penny (or any other coin, or coin-shaped object) in your penny loafers? What is the line between timeless and passé?

We invited two of GQ’s most well-heeled staffers, associate grooming and wellness editor Tyler Chin and senior style commerce writer Gerald Ortiz, to ponder the pros and cons of the penny loafer.

Mahershala Ali, an ostensible penny proponent, in London on October 19.

Tyler Chin, associate grooming and wellness editor: Sure! I’ve seen so many guys wearing loafers these days, and yet still haven’t seen any of them harking back to the shoe’s origins with a penny in them. Maybe I’m not looking hard enough or I’m not in circles where they are doing that, but it’s an inoffensive and easy way to accessorize the shoe. Plus, I had an English teacher in high school who did it, and while she wasn’t particularly cool, I did think the sartorial choice was neat.

Gerald Ortiz, senior style commerce writer: No. I feel like guys who put pennies in their loafers are either try-hards, or just learned about that particular piece of prep trivia. The only acceptable instance to wear a penny in your loafers is if you’re really about that prep/Ivy style, like Preppy Pete, or the guys who frequent Alfargo’s Market (shout out).

TC: I think the penny would be the most boring and obvious choice. If a quarter could fit in there, I think it’d be so funny if you were at a vending machine and short a few cents and settled up with the coin in your shoe. One time, the button on my pants fell off on the street and I lost it, but imagine I had a spare button in my shoe? I could reattach it right there and then! In the hypothetical I’d also need to be toting around a sewing kit, but hey, I’d love to think I could be MacGyver in that scenario.

GO: It doesn’t have to be anything at all. But if you’re going to do it, I don’t see why it couldn’t be another coin or object. It could be a Chuck E. Cheese token. Whatever you put in the slot, it would still be wack.

TC: I just got back from a trip to Tokyo, and I had a lot of leftover yen coins. Two of them—the five and 50 yen—have a hole on them, which differentiates them from your boring American coin. I’d throw one of them in my shoe and be one of those people who never stops talking about their travel abroad.

GO: I saw on Instagram someone stick a hundred dollar bill into their loafer and that was funny for a second. Then I scrolled on to the next meme.

TC: I’m pretty sure a coin that is that close to the ground will surely have a patina before the end of the day. Also, I can’t remember the last time I’ve seen a shiny new coin—who carries loose change these days anyway?!

GO: You know, it might be kind of cool to see a penny completely covered in verdigris slotted into your loafers. But maybe the coin should match the sheen of your loafers. I dunno, just don’t do it and you won’t have to think about it.

TC: Everyone I know is very familiar with my love of penny loafers. Lately I’ve been wearing them with bootcut jeans that are just a little longer than usual, so they have a nice drape over the shoe. Top-wise, I’d go with a tucked-in button up, T-shirt, or sweater. Pretty much anything that would go well with sneakers would be equally suited for wearing with loafers. Which is nearly all outfits.

GO: Penniless or not, a pair of wide khakis with a clean-to-slight break and a striped button-up is a failsafe outfit. It wouldn’t be very inventive, but it would pass muster for most conceivable situations. Vintage straight-leg jeans with good fades, a fucked-up band tee, and a sportcoat is also a great look. But the best looks are always the unexpected pairings.

TC: It’s sort of like how guys will go on and on about having functional sleeve buttons on a suit jacket. Is there any situation where you’ll actually need to unbutton those or do you just leave one or two undone to “stunt” on your unimpressed bros? If you really want to put a coin in your shoe for the sake of accessorizing your footwear, then by all means get a shoe with a functioning penny slot and use it! But if you’re doing it just so you can say “Yeah, these joints are the real deal!” and use it as an excuse to talk about the history of the shoe and why yours are superior, then I’d rather you wear lace-ups.

GO: I think it’s fine, even preferable to have a faux penny slot. It’s like how jeans still come with the watch pocket. It’s purely vestigial at this point, but would feel incomplete without it.

TC: One of these days, I’ll be telling my great-great grandchildren about the origins of the penny loafer and they’ll be confused because they would’ve never heard or seen the one-cent coin before. I’ll whip one out of my shoe and instead of being impressed they’ll chalk it up to another one of Old Man Ty’s tales from his youth, and tuck me back into the nursing home they checked me into.

GO: Lol. I guess it’ll make the penny more covetable. But it’ll be a long time before the penny becomes that desirable. That said, I love this British brand Six 95 that’s designing cool pieces using coins, so I can absolutely see more labels farming pennies for their clothes and accessories.

TC: Men will break up anything other than a toxic relationship. Loafers did nothing wrong and sometimes it’s just the person wearing them that needs to hit the curb.

GO: It makes sense. It’s like any trend, really. Even if they’re as versatile as we’ve been propping them up to be, loafers reached an inflection point where, for some guys, they’re just not the vibe anymore. But I don’t think they’ll dip so low to be thought of as cringe or as an emblem of the 2020s. They’re as classic as it gets and they’ll go through their own ebbs and flows.

TC: A quarter to increase my net worth 25-fold.

Cool adaptations: How fish survive in the Antarctic Ocean

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

With water temperatures as low as -1.9°C (28.6°F), the Southern Ocean is a place of extreme living conditions. A new study led by Senckenberg researchers shows how fish have adapted to Antarctic temperatures. The researchers used the Antarctic spiny plunderfish (Harpagifer antarcticus) as a model organism and, for the first time, analyzed the maternal and paternal copies of its genome separately. This allowed them to uncover mechanisms that contribute to this group of fishes' adaptation to extreme cold.

AMD VP and chief Zen architect rebuke claims that Nvidia and Arm made CPUs for agentic AI — ‘We didn’t magically know four years ago that agentic was going to be a thing’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

AMD’s new Venice chips aren’t agentic CPUs, but they’re the best CPUs for agentic AI, says AMD.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Two prominent voices within AMD agree that the company didn’t design its new Zen 6 family, codenamed Venice, explicitly for agentic AI in a clear shot at Nvidia and Arm, who’ve both described their latest data center CPUs as “agentic” in nature. In separate talks, AMD’s chief Zen architect Mike Clark and corporate VP Madhu Rangarajan said that the company didn’t predict the agentic wave when Zen 6 development started.

We just wrapped up testing AMD's Venice CPUs, which you can read more about in our Zen 6 benchmarking article.

CPU development cycles are measured in years, and given that the agentic frenzy around CPUs hit around March of this year, it’s unreasonable to expect that anyone has developed silicon specifically for agentic AI, much less a general-purpose data center CPU. And still, Nvidia claims its Vera CPU was “purpose-built” for agentic AI, while Arm says its AGI CPU was “designed for the demands of agentic AI.”

“We think that the EPYC 9006 CPU is the best CPU for the agentic era, and spoiler alert, we didn’t magically know four years ago that agentic was going to be a thing and purpose-built something for agentic,” said Rangarajan in a session with media. “The reality is an agentic workflow is a diverse set of tasks… and in order to execute those tasks well, you need compute that is able to handle a diverse set of workloads with a full portfolio.”

Introducing the Zen 6 microarchitecture, Mike Clark agreed with the sentiment: “I’m not going to mislead and act like I knew agentic computing was coming, or we knew it, but we did strongly believe that the CPU would always have a place in the software ecosystem of AI… And as agentic [has] blown up, we [are] able to now deploy that for agentic compute workloads.”

As AMD rolls out its next-generation microarchitecture with Venice CPUs, it’s targeting the messaging from Arm and Nvidia, which have posed the most direct threat to AMD’s continued rise in the data center on the back of agentic workloads. And indeed, there are performance vectors where Nvidia’s Vera looks strong for agentic workloads, as you can see in our Nvidia Vera testing. AMD’s argument, however, is that agentic AI calls for a broad range of CPUs just as general-purpose workloads do, pushing back on the single-SKU approach of Nvidia and Arm.

Our testing of two AMD Venice chips supports that argument, as well, with the dense EPYC 9996 with its 256 cores and 512 threads offering peak throughput performance, while the frequency-optimized EPYC 9686F goes toe-to-toe with Vera in the role of an AI host node. AMD is launching two additional ranges next year, with Venice-X targeting HPC with its 3D-stack cache, and Verano going directly after Vera as an AI host node with an LPDDR5X memory system.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Although the workload characteristics of agentic AI are still in the process of being defined, we have a better understanding of how those loops work now than we did at the beginning of the year. And like all complex topics, the answer to what is important in a CPU for agentic AI is the always-disappointing “it depends.”

As we break down in our early testing procedure for agentic AI, the workload characteristics and demand on the CPU/memory are defined by what the agent is doing, and in the data center, what several agents are doing at high concurrency when sandboxed. A coding agent will generally call more tools that are taxing on the CPU than a web browsing agent, for example, while an agent that queries a database may call for strong memory performance.

Of course, there are some vectors of performance that can’t be addressed with different SKUs (such as that strong memory performance). However, the potential diversity of agentic workloads certainly makes an argument for CPU variety. At a most basic level, an agentic loop allows the LLM to understand functions you’ve defined, and then pass arguments to those functions to execute them. We’re talking about a workload as broad as what you can define in a function in languages like Python and Rust.

AMD winds up Zen 6 cores for faster clock speeds

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Chips boast up to 256 cores, clocks up to 5.3 GHz, and IPC gains of 20-31 percent, just not all on the same SKU

After teasing its 256-core Epycs for more than a year, AMD is finally ready to talk about architectural changes to the Zen core that not only make it possible, but will eventually power its next generation of Ryzen processors in desktop and mobile PCs.

During a press event in Las Vegas last week, AMD spilled the beans on its latest Zen cores, which promise an average 27 percent uplift in instructions per clock (IPC) across a variety of AI and server workloads, along with boosted clocks ranging from 4.1 to 5.3 GHz. We expect to see even higher frequencies on consumer chips before long, but AMD isn’t quite ready to talk about Ryzen just yet.

CPU performance is a function of power, IPC, and clocks, and with gains across all three, the cores are shaping up to be one of the company’s biggest generational jumps ever.

As in past Epyc launches, Zen 6 isn’t one core. It comes in two flavors: a frequency-optimized variant (Zen 6) and a density-optimized version (Zen 6c).

This split dates back to the launch of AMD’s Bergamo Epycs in 2023, which packed 128 compact Zen 4c cores into just eight chiplets. The chip’s debut drew comparisons to Intel’s still relatively new Efficient (E-cores) for obvious reasons. But under the hood, AMD’s Zen 4c was nothing like what Intel was doing. The core itself was nearly identical to Zen 4; it simply traded clocks and a bit of cache per core for a more densely packed CPU die.

The only difference between running an application on Bergamo or Genoa was how quickly it executed. The same could not be said of Intel’s P and E cores, which used different ISAs.

This remained a defining characteristic and advantage of AMD’s compact core architecture through Zen 5, which allowed it to ship Turin parts with up to 192 cores without users having to worry about whether their app was running on a compact core or not.

With Zen 6c, at least as we understand it, this philosophy is largely unchanged. Both Zen 6 and Zen 6c share the same ISA, with clock being the main differentiator. There are other micro-architectural differences between the two cores designed to balance IPC and clock speeds more effectively.

Looking at the core’s block diagram, this isn’t immediately obvious. Zen 6 and Zen 5 share a very similar front and backend.

We'd be tempted to call the core iterative if it weren't for the 20 to 31 percent IPC uplift for cloud and AI workloads that AMD claims over Zen 5. This suggests that core for core, a 4.1 GHz Zen 6c-based Venice Epyc should deliver performance on par with a Zen 5-based Turin Epyc running at 5 GHz.

That's a pretty sizable jump, but just because AMD is seeing generational performance uplift in enterprise server and cloud workloads doesn't mean we'll see the same gains on the client side. Many will recall the roughly 5 percent performance gains gaming PC enthusiasts reported with the Ryzen 9000-series, leading to the rather unfortunate turn of phrase "Zen 5 percent."

That happened because game engines at the time simply weren't CPU-bottlenecked, and so faster cores could only raise the ceiling so much. Even so, AMD fellow and "Zen daddy" Mike Clark tells El Reg that PC buyers expect more than a 6 percent gain from Zen 6.

We're glossing over a bunch of low-level architectural optimizations here, but according to Clark, one of the biggest drivers of performance was to the front end. Zen 5 introduced a dual front end, which for simple workloads worked great, but lost a lot of steam when confronted with more dynamic loads.

"The Zen 6 team did a really good job for those larger footprint workloads that have a bipolar nature," Clark said. "It's not something that comes through. You're not going to see it in a block diagram very well. It's sort of subtle... but it's a big lever."

Another contributor was the move to a distributed scheduler in the out-of-order pipeline. One of the biggest performance drivers in modern CPU cores is that they don't execute instructions in the order they receive them. Instead, they reorder the instructions so as to keep the core's backend execution units fed with a constant stream of instructions.

This is where we see the biggest difference between Zen 6 and Zen 6c. To account for the slower clocks on the latter core, AMD gave the core's scheduler deeper queues, which Clark tells us helps with higher-latency workloads. Meanwhile, from what we understand, Zen 6's shallower queue enables it to clock higher on less power.

Finally, on the backend, AMD made several subtle changes which should have major implications for vector heavy workloads, including AI. One of the more significant was the ability to perform four AVX2 or two AVX-512 loads per cycle.

While this doesn't improve AVX-512 performance much, for applications that haven't been updated to use it, the core is now able to perform twice as many loads, which should improve throughput for legacy applications. This should come in handy, particularly on PCs, where Intel's outright lack of AVX-512 support has made AVX2 the lowest common denominator for a lot of software.

That's not to say AVX-512 didn't get some love this generation. For those running AI workloads on CPUs, Zen 6 now natively supports FP16 datatypes. Previously FP16 was supported, but required padding the data to FP32, which meant the lower precision didn't buy much in terms of performance. That's no longer the case in Zen 6.

And while we're on the topic of AI acceleration, Clark notes that Zen 6 doubled the VNNI-INT8 pipes from two to four, and should effectively double throughput for workloads that support it.

While not strictly part of the core, AMD's Zen 6 processors also address one of the biggest asymmetries associated with its frequency and density optimized cores.

Going back to Bergamo, opting for a density-optimized Epyc didn't just come at the expense of clocks, but cache too. Each Zen 4c or Zen 5c chiplet contained twice as many cores, but the same amount of L3 cache.

16 channels of DDR5, 5 GHz clock speeds, and support for CXL 3.1 memory god boxes? What's not to like?

With Zen 6 and Zen 6c, you may be giving up some clocks, but cache per core is now a constant 4 MB. As we discussed in a Venice deep dive back in September, each of the processor's eight CPU dies contain 12 or 32 cores and 48 and 128 MB respectively.

The benefit here is that users don't have to worry about different ISAs or cache hierarchies when optimizing their software stacks against AMD's Epyc processors. The same cannot be said of Intel's Xeon processors.

As we alluded to earlier, Chipzilla's performance and efficiency cores use different micro-architectures, ISAs, cache hierarchies, clocks, and even cluster their cores differently. This can pose real headaches if you have workloads that take advantage of fat vector registers like AVX-512, or accelerators like AMX, which will work just fine on Intel's P-cores but won't even run on their E-cores.

This, along with support for SMT (Hyperthreading), which Intel has dropped for now, could help the House of Zen vacuum up even more market share from its long-time rival. ®

Where to Eat During AFM 2026: Century City and Beverly Hills’ Best Restaurants

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

AFM 2026 is in the center of the independent film business dealmaking world and close to many of the entertainment biz’s favorite restaurants. Century City and nearby Beverly Hills are home to the major agencies, law firms and financing entities, which equals power lunching and see-and-be-seen gathering spots close to the confab. Throw in some legendary eateries, and AFMers are perfectly positioned to dine where Hollywood dealmakers, agency foot soldiers and bold names eat out.Century City’s dining options start at the host venue: Birdie G’s is the Fairmont Century Plaza’s new full-service restaurant. Expect classic American cooking in a relaxed setting. There will be mobile coffee stations and daily grab-and-go items near the Fairmont’s lower-level conference rooms. Choices include hearty breakfast burritos, creative salads and an array of sandwiches.At the adjacent Westfield Century City, where AFM badge holders can take advantage of a discount program, more than 65 food outlets populate the multi-level, open-air mall.Specializing in dim sum, Din Tai Fung (10250 Santa Monica Blvd., second level, dtf.com) excels in Taiwan-style, handmade soup dumplings and flavorful noodle dishes. Waits can be extensive, so sit at the bar, reserve ahead or use yelp.com to join the real-time waitlist. Related Stories Pixar Names Jonathan Garson as President; Jim Morris Moves to Emeritus Role

Terra, the restaurant at Eataly Los Angeles (10250 Santa Monica Blvd., Eataly.com), boasts ample outdoor seating to enjoy Terra’s wood-fired Italian dishes and craft cocktails. The Monday-Friday happy hour on the bar terrace features $10 glasses of wine, cocktails and Aperol spritzes paired with grilled appetizers.

On the lower level of the nearby Century Building, Hinoki & the Bird (10 W. Century Dr., hinokiandthebird.com) attracts the high-powered lunch crowd, including CAA agents along with their clients and execs from the Fox studio lot. Serving globally inspired California cuisine, lunch favorites range from sashimi starters to the ever-popular crispy jidori chicken to the kale salad topped with spiced cashews. Enticing dinner entrees include the hinoki-scented black cod and dry-aged Peking duck. The tucked-away location provides privacy; the sheltered, heated outdoor patio is open year-round. Reservations are highly recommended. Monday-Friday happy hour runs 5-7 p.m.

Beverly Hills excels at chic hotel dining. Opened in May, Gemma on the rooftop of the Waldorf Astoria Beverly Hills (9850 Wilshire Blvd., waldorfastoriabeverlyhills.com) has unequaled views across Beverly Hills. The upscale pan-Asian fare (highlights: dim sum, seafood raw bar, lobster burger and Singaporean noodles) and fanciful cocktails more than satisfy the rooftop’s stylish crowd. Edoardo Baldi’s Tuscan steakhouse Baldi (baldi.com) brings the restaurateur’s signature hospitality and famed pasta to the Waldorf’s first floor.

The Maybourne Beverly Hills (225 N. Canon Dr., maybournebeverlyhills.com) boasts a caviar and oyster bar at its cozy Maybourne Bar. Tradition and old-school service reign on the leafy patio of the Beverly Hills Hotel’s Polo Lounge (9641 Sunset Blvd., dorchestercollection.com), a go-to for Hollywood’s elite for decades.

Mastro’s is celebrating 25 years, and the Garden at the Penthouse at Mastro’s Steakhouse Beverly Hills (246 Canon Dr., mastrosrestaurants.com) welcomes power players like Michael B. Jordan, Emily Blunt and John Krasinski to its clubby third-floor indoor-outdoor dining room. For dinner, there are massive steaks and chops and a heady assortment of seafood.

For those seeking a flowery, photo-ready indoor-outdoor space that feels miles away, take the world’s prettiest elevator to the rooftop restaurant Monsieur Dior by Dominique Crenn (323 N. Rodeo Dr., monsieurdiorbeverlyhills.com). The inventive lunch, dinner and lounge menus come from Michelin-starred restaurateur/chef Dominique Crenn, paired with elegant interiors by architect Peter Marino. Sip on a glass of Chateau Minuty, the house rosé wine from Provence, France, to embrace the French ambiance.

The businessperson’s lunch crowd flocks to Avra Beverly Hills (233 N. Beverly Dr., theavragroup.com) for the prix fixe, three-course Mediterranean-style lunch special priced at $41.95. Start with a Greek salad, then move on to the grilled salmon and then finish with a baklava, all served in Avra’s bright, open-air setting populated by the city’s elite.

XRP broke through support at $1.10 and dropped to around $1.04, leaving it less than 5% above the key $1 level. The Relative Strength Index has sunk to 25.8, deep in oversold territory, but buyers have yet to turn the trend. If XRP loses $1, the next major support sits near $0.90. Prices can differ by exchange, with Binance support levels running about $0.10 lower. XRP is now in a 30% correction. That happened this summer, when XRP fell to $0.70 before rebounding 53%. The 30-day funding sum currently stands at +0.18%. Spot XRP ETFs also had one of their worst days of the year, with net outflows of 9.37 million XRP, worth about $10.4 million.

Chelsea boss Xabi Alonso called out for 'worrying' Estêvão Willian decision

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Paul Merson believes Xabi Alonso "played into Sunderland's hands" on Sunday and also criticized the Chelsea manager for not starting Estêvão Willian.

Despite delivering one of his finest displays in a Blues shirt just eight days earlier during the 2-0 victory over Manchester United at Stamford Bridge, Estêvão started on the bench at the Stadium of Light. The Brazilian was introduced at halftime to replace Levi Colwill, who endured a difficult opening 45 minutes up against Black Cats striker Brian Brobbey.

The 19-year-old was unable to turn the tide for the visiting side in the second half, as Sunderland secured a vital three points, via football.london. Lifelong Chelsea supporter Merson feels Alonso got his team selection badly wrong by leaving the teenager out of the starting lineup.

Ryan Reynolds and Rob Mac address Wrexham executive's abrupt exit

USMNT legend Alexi Lalas on Mauricio Pochettino to Man Utd links amidst pressure on Michael Carrick

-Credit:Lee Parker - CameraSport, CameraSport via Getty Images

Writing in his Sky Sports column, the former Arsenal midfielder said: "I thought Xabi Alonso played into Sunderland's hands, if I'm being honest. He went there and showed them a little bit more respect than what I thought he should have.

"They were bottom of the league with five points, and they were bottom of the league with five points for a reason.

"Do you know what really worried me? Estêvão was Chelsea's best player by a million miles last week against Manchester United - especially in the first half when they were really bad. They should have been 2-0 down at half-time.

"But all of a sudden, they go to Sunderland and he's not playing. And he was missed. He's someone who can take the ball up the pitch for them.

"Alonso had to pick Estêvão, play the same team they put out against Man Utd and get him to run with the ball. Instead, he tried to make it tight, and make sure they don't get beat over winning the game."

To listen to the latest episode of 'All Out Soccer' - CLICK HERE

Alonso has handed Estêvão a Premier League start on just one occasion this season, fueling speculation regarding the Brazilian's future at the club — though such talk has somewhat subsided following his appearance against Manchester United.

Chelsea host Leeds United at Stamford Bridge on November 21, their first fixture following the final international break of 2026. Estêvão, meanwhile, has been named in Carlo Ancelotti's latest Brazil squad for upcoming friendlies against Japan and Singapore.

Despite limited playing time at Stamford Bridge, Ancelotti has urged Estêvão to seize his opportunity on the international stage: "We hope he can show all the quality he has. We'll keep an eye on him.

"If he doesn't play a single minute at Chelsea, we'll keep an eye on him until the day we think he's no longer useful for the national team."

Brazil's second friendly, scheduled for November 17 in Singapore at the National Stadium in Kallang, poses a logistical headache for Estêvão, with the roughly 15-hour return flight to London set to significantly disrupt his preparations ahead of the Leeds clash.

Mamdani makes behind-the-scenes moves to shape 2028 Democratic presidential race

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Politics  |  Read Source →

Asked about 2028, progressive champion Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez of New York brushes aside political speculation, says voters want Democrats in Congress focused on governing.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani is quietly working towards a new goal: to become a progressive kingmaker in the burgeoning battle for the 2028 Democratic presidential nomination.

The socialist mayor of the nation's most populous city, who can't run for president himself because he's not a natural-born U.S. citizen, has been meeting behind closed doors with labor leaders to highlight the need for a union-aligned or left-wing candidate to seek the presidency.

The mayor's moves come as he's reportedly said he's uncertain whether progressive champion Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez of New York, his close political ally, will run for the White House.

According to Axios, which was first to report the news, Mamdani is concerned that if Ocasio-Cortez stays out of the 2028 race, it could leave progressives without a standard-bearer to face off against a more centrist presidential front-runner in the primary battle.

WILL TRUMP SIT OUT MAKING A 2028 ENDORSEMENT IN THE RACE TO SUCCEED HIM?

Then-New York City mayoral candidate Zohran Mamdani holds hands with Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez (D-NY) at the end of a campaign rally at Forest Hills Stadium in the Queens borough of New York City on October 26, 2025 (ANGELA WEISS/AFP via Getty Images)

Mamdani, who is seen as a major powerbroker for the left-wing of the Democratic Party after his historic 2025 mayoral election victory, argues that the progressive movement can't waste their momentum coming out of last week's trouncing of Republicans in the midterm elections, as Democrats won a massive House majority and control of the Senate.

A source familiar with Mamdani's 2028 conversations told Axios the mayor "wants the progressive ecosystem to mobilize quickly, strategically and efficiently if a moderate candidate is clearly on the rise,"

Meanwhile, Ocasio-Cortez is publicly pumping the brakes on speculation she'll launch a presidential campaign or bid for the Senate in New York, even as a newly formed group of activists is urging her run for the White House.

VANCE, CRUZ, DESANTIS DIAGNOSE GOP'S MIDTERM DISASTER AS 2028 BATTLE BEGINS

Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez, D-N.Y., departs following a vote at the US Capitol in Washington, DC, US, on Wednesday, July 22, 2026. (Aaron Schwartz/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

"I think the thing that's important here is the last thing that people want to hear from me or any other politician here is about the next election. And what they actually want to hear from us is about what we are going to do with the electoral results they just gave us on Tuesday,' Ocasio-Cortez told reporters Monday night, as she pointed to the Democrats' trouncing of Republicans in the midterms, as the party won a massive House majority and control of the Senate.

The comments from Ocasio-Cortez, who was re-elected last week to a fifth term representing parts of New York City, came hours after the formal launch of Ready for AOC, a political group working to encourage her to run for president by showing her she has legions of supporters ready to mobilize on her behalf.

"A people-powered movement asking Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez to run for President in 2028. No campaign. No money. Just names," is how the group described itself on social media.

But Ocasio-Cortez said that voters are more concerned about congressional Democrats' immediate legislative agenda rather than speculating about the next election.

"I think people are less concerned about the horse race, and they want to know: What are the investigations we're going to be starting? What is the legislation that we're going to be starting?" she said.

Veteran Democratic strategist David Plouffe, campaign manager for former President Barack Obama's landmark 2008 White House victory and a senior adviser on then-Vice President Kamala Harris' 2024 bid, said last week, "If AOC runs, she's going to start, 20-25%, probably. The question is, can she add to that? If she doesn't run, then that is wide open. That's where I think someone like Abdul may say 'even though I just won, should I look at it?'"

Democratic Senate nominee from Michigan Abdul El-Sayed celebrates his victory during an election night event with Michigan Democrats in Detroit, Michigan, US, early on Wednesday, Nov. 4, 2026. (Sarah Rice/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

Plouffe, who was speaking at a forum hosted by Harvard University's Institute of Politics, was referring to Democratic Sen.-elect Abdul El-Sayed of Michigan, who made history last week as the first Muslim elected to the U.S. Senate.

"That's the thing about this race. It's so wide open that there may be some people run who surprise us. I also think they'll be some people who don't run who'll surprise us," Plouffe added.

Ocasio-Cortez said in September that she's weighing what to do next, whether it be a White House run or a bid for the seat currently held by Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer, a fellow Democrat and New Yorker.

"I know there is a lot of speculation about what I will decide to do in 2028. I get asked about it nearly every interview and event," Ocasio-Cortez wrote in a fundraising email to supporters.

The now-37-year-old lawmaker with a massive social media footprint said in a New York Times interview two months ago that her decision, which could happen sometime after the midterm elections, would come down to answering the question, "Where can I do the most good?"

Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez, D-N.Y., and Senate Majority Leader Sen. Chuck Schumer, D-N.Y., speak on Capitol Hill on Aug. 3, 2025. (Kent Nishimura/Los Angeles Times via Getty Images)

Schumer, who turns 76 later this month, has left his 2028 plans open and has not announced whether he’ll run for sixth six-year term in the Senate.

Ocasio-Cortez isn't the only leading figure on the left who is mulling a presidential run. So is Rep. Ro Khanna of California, a top House progressive.

Allies of the soon-to-be six-term congressman last week launched a draft committee called "Run Ro" to build support and raise funds for a potential 2028 presidential campaign.

Paul Steinhauser covers the national campaign trail from coast to coast for Fox News

Goldman, a lead bank on Anthropic’s IPO, sees $685B in revenue by 2030

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The forecast arrives as Anthropic starts selling its IPO to investors at a value of up to $1.4T.

Goldman Sachs analysts expect Anthropic’s revenue to reach about $685B by 2030, Bloomberg reported. Goldman is one of the banks leading Anthropic’s IPO, which began marketing on Tuesday at a value of up to $1.4T and is due to price on 18 November.

Goldman Sachs analysts expect Anthropic’s revenue to reach about $685B by 2030, nearly 150 times its sales last year, Bloomberg reported, citing a person familiar with the estimates. The Financial Times reported some of the figures earlier.

Goldman is one of the banks leading Anthropic’s IPO, which began marketing to investors on Tuesday. Anthropic is selling shares at $70 to $78 each to raise up to $64.4B, or $90.4B with a $26B private placement from Google, Nvidia and others, TheWrap reported.

That values Anthropic at up to $1.4T, counting stock options. The shares are due to price on 18 November and start trading on Nasdaq the next day under the ticker ANT.

Goldman’s analysts see $586B a year coming from business customers by 2030, and about $100B from everyone else. They expect Anthropic’s spending on computing to rise more than tenfold to nearly $400B a year. Anthropic would only start making positive free cash flow in 2030, they said.

The forecast runs well ahead of Anthropic’s own. Its internal forecasts reportedly pointed to $190B to $200B in revenue in 2028. Sales are growing fast, though, reaching nearly $17B in the third quarter alone.

Not everyone is convinced. Research firm New Constructs has called it the “most ridiculous IPO of 2026” and valued Anthropic at as little as $144B. Goldman and Anthropic declined to comment to Bloomberg.

I write about startups, venture capital, digital policy, and Europe ecosystem, with an eye on the larger story beneath them: who gets to build the future, who profits from it, and how Europe is learning to speak in a louder voice of its own. Before moving into senior editorial leadership, I've built my career for over +10 years across journalism, storytelling, content strategy, SEO, and digital publishing, with experience in SaaS, hospitality, art, and culture.

Latin Grammy Awards 2026 Predictions: Who Will Win the Top Honors?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Billboard's Latin and Español editors offer an analysis of the likely winners and deserving contenders across the top four categories.

Who will win the top prize at the 2026 Latin Grammys? Every year, Billboard Latin and Billboard Español editors dissect the top categories — album of the year, song of the year, record of the year and best new artist — to forecast the winners and spotlight the most deserving artists.

With the ceremony just around the corner this Thursday (Nov. 12), our editorial team has engaged in an analysis, considering market trends and historical voting patterns to make informed predictions. This discussion includes insights from Sigal Ratner-Arias, deputy editor of Billboard Español; Jessica Roiz, associate editor for Latin; and Ingrid Fajardo, senior social media manager and staff writer.

On its 26th annual edition, the Latin Grammy Awards ceremony will broadcast live from the MGM Grand Garden Arena in Las Vegas. The three-hour telecast will be produced by TelevisaUnivision and air across the network’s U.S. platforms, beginning at 8 p.m. ET. It will be preceded by the Latin Grammy Premiere, where the majority of the categories are awarded.

To refresh your memory, here’s this year’s full list of nominees. Below are our predictions for the general categories:

Jessica Roiz: All are great works, but the clear winner in my book is Milo J’s La Vida Era Más Corta. In 15 songs, the then 18-year-old bridged the past, present and future, most notably with his star-studded collaborations that include Trueno, Akriila, Silvio Rodríguez and the late Mercedes Sosa. Showcasing the maturity beyond his years, the Argentine artist — who’s one of the youngest to be nominated in this coveted category — beautifully captures Latin America’s rich folk traditions, where chacareras, zambas, chamamés and tangos effortlessly coexist.

Sigal Ratner-Arias: I agree that all of these are excellent and that Milo — despite his young age — has created a truly extraordinary album. However, I don’t see any other winner here besides Rosalía for LUX, a complex, one-of-a-kind masterpiece. Recorded with the London Symphony Orchestra and featuring songs musically and lyrically rich — in 13 different languages! — Rosalía not only expanded the meaning of what a pop album can be, she also reached new vocal heights with an impressive operatic work.

Ingrid Fajardo: I agree with Jessica and Sigal; we definitely saw masterpieces in this category, including Milo J’s La Vida Era Más Corta and Carín León’s MUDA. But Rosalía took us into her world with LUX. For me, it’s a work of art; it feels like reaching the deepest part of her musical thoughts, her knowledge and the art she carries inside, captured in this 18-track album combining a masterful symphony orchestra perfectly intertwined with pop that makes you feel the performance of each song.

Jessica Roiz: Traditionally, the song of the year honors the musical and lyrical composition of a song, which is why I would love to see “Coleccionando Heridas” by Karol G and Marco Antonio Solís nab the award this year. There’s something about the nostalgia aspect of this song that has me in a chokehold, from its grupero love ballad melody to its heartfelt and relatable lyrics. “Coleccionando Heridas” (collecting wounds) puts on the forefront the modern-day dating life that can be quite challenging.

Sigal Ratner-Arias: “Coleccionando Heridas” is also one of my favorites on this list, and if it wins, it would be well-deserved, no doubt. However, I think Rosalía could easily take home this award with “La Perla” alongside Yahritza y Su Esencia. The song, with its brilliantly ironic lyrics about a manipulative ex-partner, has become an anthem of catharsis, also driven by an exhilarating orchestral arrangement and the powerful voices of Rosi and Yahritza.

Ingrid Fajardo: This is a tough category, as many nominees tackle more traditional themes in their lyrics. For example, Karol G’s “Coleccionando Heridas” delivers a direct message about love and heartbreak, while Rosalía’s “La Perla”, which could be described as “communal therapy,” brilliantly captures the characteristics of a narcissistic person. Milo J’s “Solifican12″ stands out with its folk-inspired sound and introspective verses, using metaphor to explore personal transformation. For its depth and originality, I’d love to see “Solifican12” take home the Latin Grammy.

Roiz: Every song in this category is a true masterpiece, but I’m personally torn between two songs that are complete opposites, but impactful in their own right. The very motivational “Alma” not only introduced an edgy, confident and fearless side to Elena Rose, but its acoustic and minimalist soundscape truly redefined today’s Latin pop music scene. Meanwhile, Rosalía’s “La Perla” featuring Yahritza y Su Esencia is an elegant melting pot that laces ranchera, classical symphony and waltz music, backed by the power vocals of the Spanish singer-songwriter and the Mexican band’s lead Yahrtiza Martinez. Now, given the fact that the record of the year recognizes the overall audio recording and sonic performance of a track, I highly believe “La Perla” can take the Latin Grammy award this year.

Ratner-Arias: I love all the songs and artists in this category, but again, I feel that the award here will come down to “La Perla” and “Coleccionando Heridas.” For the same reasons I explained before, I’m leaning toward Rosalía taking this one as well.

Ingrid Fajardo: For me, it’s definitely Rosalía’s “La Perla,” featuring Yahritza y Su Esencia. The song opens with a gentle, traditional guitar, setting an intimate atmosphere that draws the listener in. As the track unfolds, violins gradually layer in, their melodies weaving around the vocals and deepening the emotional resonance. Initially, there’s a light, playful energy to the arrangement, but as the song progresses, the orchestration projects, and a full symphony emerges, infusing the piece with an unexpected intensity and dramatic power.

Roiz: Macario Martínez. There’s something so heartwarming and honest in his artistry that I would be very excited if he won best new artist. At 25 years old, the Mexican folk artist is an old-soul whose music is rooted in intimate lyrics and acoustic melodies, backed by his tender-yet-soulful vocals. What makes Martínez an even more charming and compelling artist is his success story from working as a street sweeper in Mexico to landing his first Latin Grammy nomination. Winning would only give more power to the viral track that got him here today: “Sueña Lindo, Corazón.” Talk about a full-circle moment.

Ratner-Arias: I agree that it would be wonderful if Macario won — and well-deserved, too. That said, it’s been a long time since I’ve seen this much excitement in the industry surrounding one new artist — and that is Aria Vega. Our Latin Up-And-Comer for the month of July has stood out this year in a way few debut artists do, connecting with audiences not only through her refreshing fusion of urban and Caribbean rhythms but also through her infectious joy and unmatched charisma. I believe this costeñita from Barranquilla is well on her way to becoming a true superstar, and is the one to beat here.

Ingrid Fajardo: Aria is truly having a remarkable year. In February 2026, I featured her on Billboard‘s On the Radar Latin, and just five months later she was named our Latin Up-And-Comer. Audiences have genuinely connected to her essence and the fresh perspective she brings to Latin music. But I also want to highlight Macario Martínez, whose journey is a true testament to the power of dreams. He began with a viral video, just him, his music, and a heartfelt plea for listeners’ support. What started as a simple, authentic moment blossomed into a story with a happy ending. Throughout his rise, Macario has stayed true to his roots, maintaining a sound that feels like that of an old soul, someone who has returned to share his music and allow others to see the world through his eyes. In many ways, he reminds me of Juan Gabriel through his music, carrying his Mexican heritage with a very nostalgic sound.

Christensen Arms Ridgeline FFT in 300 PRC Rifle, Expert Texted and Reviewed

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

This article was produced in partnership with Christensen Arms

We’ve looked at the Christensen Arms Ridgeline FFT rifle in the past. In fact, it was selected as our editor’s choice during our annual 2025 rifle test. The rifle is offered in a plethora of cartridges, and while cartridge choice is not the most important aspect in a rifle a hunter might plan to use all over the world, it does matter. Several rifle cartridges qualify as world-wide big game cartridges, and while magnum performance is not mandatory, many prefer that extra power for assurance at distance, especially for larger game. With that as a guiding principle I decided to revisit the Christensen Arms Ridgeline FFT chambered in 300 PRC to see how it might fill this anywhere and everything category.

The two most distinguishing characteristics of the Ridgeline FFT are its carbon fiber wrapped barrel and its carbon fiber stock. What many hunters and shooters do not realize is that Christensen Arms pioneered the carbon fiber wrapped barrel. The Ridgeline’s barrel is made of 416 stainless-steel, and it is button-rifled and hand-honed at the factory. Christensen Arms also contours the barrel with a proprietary stepped process that reduces in diameter from the ends to the center, then they wrapped the barrel various layers of carbon fiber. In 300 PRC the barrel is 22 inches long, has a 1 in 8 rifling twist, and is threaded at 5/8×24. A thread protector is provided but the rifle ships with a stainless steel side baffle muzzle brake.

The carbon stock—offered in a variety of cool looking camo patterns—on the Ridgeline FFT is just as proprietary as the barrel and Christensen Arms builds them at their factory in Gunnison, Utah. FFT stands for “Flash Forged Technology,” and this stock is of a monocoque design, which means there is no internal support structure. It’s outfitted with carbon fiber pillars that the action screws pass through, it’s glass bedded in the recoil lug area, and is relieved to provide a free-floating barrel. With the buttpad installed the stock weighs only about 20 ounces, which is light even by modern synthetic stock standards. It also has a hinged aluminum floor plate but is compatible with aftermarket AICS detachable magazine conversion kits.

The Ridgeline FFT utilizes Christensen Arms Model 14 action, which is a variation on the Remington 700 action, and it will accept Remington 700 scope bases and mounts. This is a two-lug action with a 90° throw, but that’s where the similarities end. The bolt release on the Model 14 action is located on the left side of the action, the bolt is spiral fluted, and its outfitted with a much more robust and reliable M16-style extractor. Inside the bolt face there are redundant—two—spring loaded plunger ejectors. The handle on the bolt is oversized and grooved, and the bolt handle is skeletonized for looks and to reduce weight. And finally, out of the box this rifle comes with a very good TriggerTech trigger that’s user adjustable. Every Ridgeline FFT rifle also comes with Christensen Arms sub-MOA accuracy guarantee.

From a muzzle velocity standpoint, the 300 Precision Rifle Cartridge (PRC) falls about in the middle of Magnum 0.30-caliber rifle cartridges. Introduced in 2018 by Hornady, the 300 PRC is not the fastest 0.30 caliber. It has about a 50 to 100 fps advantage over the 300 Winchester Magnum but it’s about 100 to 150 fps behind the 300 Weatherby Magnum and the 300 Remington Ultra Magnum, respectively. However, the 300 PRC performs very well at distance because of its 1 turn in 8.5-inch rifling twist rate which allows it to shoot very aero dynamic high ballistic coefficient bullets that retain velocity better and drift less in the wind.

For example, zeroed at 200 yards Hornady’s 212-grian ELD-X load for the 300 PRC will only drop about 38 inches at 500 yards, where it will still impact at 2200 fps and with 2304 foot pounds of kinetic energy. A 200-grain bullet from the 300 Winchester Magnum cannot match this, and you must step up to the 27% harder recoiling 220-grain bullet in a 300 Remington Ultra Magnum to match this performance. This all makes the 300 PRC a great choice for hunters who want to deliver maximum power at distance. There’s also about 20 factory loads offered for the 300 PRC.

I mounted one of the new Zeiss V3 riflescopes to the supplied 20 MOA rail that comes with the rifle using Leupold Backcountry rings. Even then, the rifle weighed less than seven pounds. Four loads were tested by firing three, five-shot groups from a sandbag rest at 100 yards with each load. In total, 12, five-shot groups were fired and the average for all those groups was 1.23 inches. All this shooting was conducted with the side baffle muzzle brake installed.

Christensen offers a sub-MOA precision guarantee with the Ridgeline FFT rifle, and this translates to an assurance the rifle can put three shots into a group measuring less than roughly an inch at 100 yards. Of course, as with any precision guarantee this assumes a capable shooter is behind the rifle and that quality ammunition is being used. Our standard precision testing always relies on five-shot groups, so no three-shot groups were measured. However, in most every case the first three shots with the 190-grain Outfitter and 210-grain Terminal Ascent loads would most likely have fallen within that sub-MOA measurement.

I’m not a fan of muzzle brakes because to me, they’re obnoxiously loud and disconcerting, and the one provided by Christensen was all of that. But at the expense of excessive muzzle blast, it was very effective at reducing recoil to what I would consider 30-06 Springfield and maybe even 308 Winchester levels. That’s notable considering the 300 PRC generates more than twice the recoil force of both cartridges. I also did some shooting with two different suppressors which also reduced recoil, but neither offered as much relief as the muzzle brake.

The Ridgeline FFT is balanced well for field shooting, either off hand or from shooting sticks. The action was smooth and it fed, fired and ejected every load tried with perfect reliability. I had two complaints with the rifle. The first is that in 300 PRC it has a limited magazine capacity of two, which allows for a fully loaded capacity of three. The second complaint is that the two position safety does not lock the bolt handle down when it’s in the “safe” position.

During a visit to the factory a few years back I got to assist with building a Ridgeline FFT chambered for the 6.5 PRC. That rifle and I have spent a lot of time together on the range and in the field. In fact, I took it to Africa in 2025 and it’s the rifle my wife used. I used another Ridgeline FFT chambered in 7mm Backcountry. So, I’ve got some experience hunting with the platform. Aside from the non-bolt locking safety I think the Ridgeline FFT is one of the best modern lightweight bolt action rifles currently offered. It delivers a great balance of performance and weight, especially at its price point. There’s no question that one in 300 PRC could effectively serve a big game hunter all over the world, and if the 300 PRC is not your cup of tea there are near two dozen other chamberings to choose from.

Forget Mayo: Spread This Rich Ingredient On Bread For A More Flavorful Grilled Cheese

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Butter gives your grilled cheese great richness, and using mayo makes it far crispier, but neither adds a ton of strong flavors that can shine through high-quality cheese. That's why you need toum, a Lebanese garlic spread that delivers intense taste with very little effort.

Toum is as rich as mayonnaise and as light and fluffy as whipped cream, created by smashing garlic into a paste and then gradually whipping in oil until it has an airy but glossy consistency. For texture, its incredible fluffiness makes it easy to slather on, covering every last inch in a swiftly melting browning agent that makes the bread crispy. But for flavoring, it makes the end product taste like garlic bread, full of rich fats and pungent aromatics that only requires a swift spread with a knife. It may seem like you're using a lot and will over-season your sandwich, but since so much of it is air, you're actually only using a moderate amount that's quite easy to thoroughly apply.

The combination of melty gooeyness inside and crisp, garlicky exteriors is a second-to-none combination that pairs well with a wide variety of cheeses. Add in the sharpness of the lemon juice, and you get something that can brighten up even the heaviest, fat-laden cheeses quite well. The only caveat is that all that garlic gives it a fairly cool smoke point, so you'll need to use low to medium heat to avoid making it grow bitter.

Since its main flavors are garlic and lemon, toum isn't quite as universal a pairing as mayonnaise or butter. It goes great with anything semi-hard and creamy, but its intensity may clash with anything stronger. You'll also want to know the type of oil used in your toum. Something made with grapeseed, canola, or other neutral varieties is far more compatible than a spread made with a flavorful extra virgin olive oil.

Cheeses with a nuttier profile, like Jarlsberg, Gruyère, and Manchego, are all solid pairings. They tend to be a bit earthier, grounding the brightness of the toum, so it isn't too intense. Since it has no eggs, you can even combine it with melted vegan cheese since nutritional yeast is typically quite aromatic and nutty. Richer, well-rounded varieties can also be quite nice, so some shreds of gouda, raclette, and any triple-cream variety are always welcome additions.

Flavored cheeses are one of the easiest ways to upgrade any melty sandwich, but not all are compatible with toum. While something rich in herbs or wine can complement the garlic quite well, a variety with a lot of sweetened fruit or more lemon can be a bit much. Since toum is already quite tangy, you may also want to steer clear of acidic cheeses like chèvre, feta, or cream cheese. While some may like the brightness, it can quickly become overly intense, and you may find yourself making a new sandwich.

Credit card debt climbs to a record $1.29 trillion, New York Fed research finds

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Americans are increasingly turning to credit cards as financial pressures mount, new reports show.

Collectively, credit card balances climbed by $22 billion in the third quarter to a record $1.29 trillion, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of New York's latest household debt report, released Tuesday. Balances are up 4.2% from a year earlier.

Total household debt, including mortgages, auto loans and student loans, also expanded by $334 billion for the July-to-September period to a record $19.1 trillion.

"Overall debt has been growing," the New York Fed researchers said on a press call Tuesday. "This does reflect financial hardship out there."

The average credit card balance per consumer now stands at $6,678, up 2.4% year over year, a separate quarterly credit industry insights report from TransUnion found. The number of people with credit cards rose 2% to 178 million from 174.8 million.

Consumers often turn to credit cards in part because they are more accessible than other types of loans — even though the average interest rate on a new credit card offer is about 24%, according to data from LendingTree. Credit card APRs vary depending on the type of card, the issuer and the borrower's creditworthiness.

"Credit cards continue to be one of the most expensive ways to borrow," said Charlie Wise, TransUnion's senior vice president of global research and consulting.

Over time, an increasing share of new credit cards have gone to borrowers with lower incomes and credit scores, and "those accounts are now showing higher levels of distress," the New York Fed researchers said in a corresponding blog post.

After credit card issuers loosened their lending standards during the Covid pandemic, delinquency rates rose in the years that followed, the New York Fed researchers said on the press call.

"Macroeconomic headwinds — namely high inflation and rising borrowing costs — have squeezed consumers, with inflation outpacing wage growth for low-income brackets," according to the New York Fed.

Meanwhile, the cost of living continues to climb, with Americans paying more for essentials such as groceries and gas.

Stubborn inflation has now stretched on for years, putting many households in an affordability squeeze. Costs for goods and services rose more than expected again in October, the Bureau of Labor Statistics also reported Tuesday.

"Higher prices and higher rates have pushed more people into debt," said Ted Rossman, principal consumer finance analyst at Money Management International, a nonprofit credit counseling agency.

Balances have continued to swell in the wake of the pandemic, and even though the growth rate is now slowing, "from a household finance standpoint, a lot of damage has been done," Rossman said.

The reports come on the heels of the Democrats' strong midterm results, when many voters expressed their dissatisfaction with the U.S. economy, according to Kyle Moore, chief economist at the nonpartisan think tank The Century Foundation.

"The throughline connecting so many of Tuesday's outcomes was a recognition that the American economy is failing American families," Moore said, referring to Election Day.

This 4-Week Workout Program Uses Just 1 Dumbbell to Help You Get Stronger

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Build muscle and fry fat over this month-long workout plan.

YES, YOU CAN work every muscle in your body with one piece of gear. We love kettlebells, med balls, and resistance bands, but if we had to choose one piece of equipment, it would be a single dumbbell.

The game plan: Three times a week, you’ll grab the simplest tool in the gym and use it to build muscle and banish fat in a super-dense half-hour workout. It’s the most effective way to fry body fat, and it’s the perfect way to train for the holidays. It’s also exactly how Hollywood-based trainer Ben Bruno, C.F.S.C., guides time-strapped A-listers like Justin Timberlake and NBA star Klay Thompson. “They want quick, efficient workouts that hit everything,” Bruno says. “So we need to move fast.”

It doesn’t get more efficient than this light-speed, one-dumbbell workout.

Stand holding a dumbbell at your left shoulder, feet shoulder-width apart, knees slightly bent, glutes squeezed, and core tight. Keeping your chest level, straighten your arm to press the dumbbell overhead (b). Pause, then slowly lower the dumbbell back to your shoulder. That’s 1 rep; do 8 per arm.

Stand holding a dumbbell at your chest. Tighten your core and step back about three feet with your right leg. Keeping your chest up and the dumbbell high, bend your knees and lower your torso until your left thigh is parallel to the floor. Your right knee should be a few inches from the floor. Pause, then push off your right leg and stand back up. That’s 1 rep; do 10 per leg.

Eb says: Squeeze your glutes when you stand up. This will help you avoid lower-back pain when doing lunges.

Set a bench to a 45-degree angle and position yourself so your chest is against it; squeeze your abs and glutes. With your left arm out at shoulder height, hold a dumbbell in your right hand, arm hanging naturally. Squeeze your shoulder blades. This is the start. Keeping your shoulders and hips steady, row the dumbbell to your right hip. Pause, then return to the start.That’s 1 rep; do 10 per arm.

Eb says: This is more than a back move; your glutes and abs should be into it, too. Tighten them up so your torso doesn’t tip down to the side of the weight.

Lie on your back, holding a dumbbell in your left hand, arm straight, directly over your chest. Press your lower back into the floor and lift your legs slightly off the floor; lift your shoulder blades slightly, too. This is the start.Bend at the elbow and shoulder, lowering the dumbbell to your chest without losing torso position. Press back to the start. That’s 1 rep; do 8 per arm.

Eb says: Struggling with the position? Bend your knees to make it easier.

Lie on your back, knees bent and feet flat on the floor. Lift your right foot off the floor and tuck your right knee toward your chest. Tighten your core. Driving your left heel into the ground, push your lower back off the floor; squeeze your left glute as you do this. Pause, then return to the start. That’s 1 rep; do 10 per side.

Set up in pushup position, glutes and abs tight, right hand gripping a dumbbell. Squeeze your shoulder blades. Pull the dumbbell off the floor, rowing it toward your rib cage but keeping your hips and shoulders square. Pause. Return the dumbbell to the start. That’s 1 rep; do 8 per arm.

Eb says: Focus on flexing the oblique on the rowing side as hard as you canto keep your hips level.

This article was originally published in 2019.

Ebenezer Samuel, C.S.C.S., is the fitness director of Men's Health and a certified trainer with more than 10 years of training experience. He's logged training time with NFL athletes and track athletes and his current training regimen includes weight training, HIIT conditioning, and yoga. Before joining Men's Health, he served as a sports columnist and tech columnist for the New York Daily News.

Why SpaceX Could Be the Best Buy-and-Hold Stock of the Decade

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SpaceX is building multiple engines that could power the company's long-term growth.

Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX -0.66%) reported $14.6 billion in Q3 revenue, a 161% increase from the prior-year period. Net income reached $4.4 billion, compared with a $1.1 billion loss a year earlier. These are extraordinary numbers for a company that went public just five months ago. And while SpaceX is best known for launching rockets, its long-term growth prospects extend well beyond the space industry. Between its expanding Starlink satellite broadband network, reusable rockets, and growing artificial intelligence (AI) business, SpaceX could gain significant ground over the next decade.

SpaceX's connectivity business, which includes Starlink, generated $5.25 billion in third-quarter revenue, up 69% year over year, and operating income jumped 99% to approximately $2.37 billion.

Unlike other satellite network operators, SpaceX both manufactures and launches its own satellites. Its reusable rockets let the company deploy additional capacity without the need to rely on an external launch provider.

Starlink also serves businesses, airlines, ships, and government customers, giving it plenty of opportunities beyond providing residential internet service. Make no mistake: Its potential customer base remains substantial, particularly in regions where conventional broadband infrastructure is limited or unavailable. And demand is likely to remain strong for at least the next 10 to 12 years.

SpaceX is also becoming a major artificial intelligence company as it expands into AI infrastructure and software. Its "super intelligence" segment actually helped drive third-quarter revenue growth, adding $6.7 billion compared with the prior-year period.

The company also expanded its computing infrastructure to 1.7 gigawatts of installed capacity as of Sept. 30. That gives SpaceX exposure to two rapidly expanding markets: satellite communications and artificial intelligence.

Management is pursuing a plan to deploy space-based computing infrastructure, too, although that remains an ambitious and expensive undertaking with considerable technical uncertainty. Still, SpaceX already has something many emerging technology companies lack: substantial revenues and profits.

SpaceX went public in June at $135 per share, valuing the company at approximately $1.77 trillion. That's an enormous valuation, even for a business growing as quickly as SpaceX. The company is also spending aggressively to expand its infrastructure. In the third quarter, its capital expenditures reached approximately $19.6 billion. These investments could support future growth, but they also create financial risks if demand disappoints or major projects take longer than expected to complete.

Competition in AI, regulatory challenges, satellite replacement costs, and the expense of developing its Starship rocket platform could also put pressure on the company's profitability in the future. And don't assume SpaceX will continue to grow at the extraordinary rate it did in the third quarter for any great length of time.

That said, the combination of SpaceX's position in commercial spaceflight, its rapidly expanding satellite communications network, and its substantial AI business is one that no other company can match. If management can sustain strong revenue growth while improving cash generation, SpaceX could reward patient shareholders over the next decade.

Ausilio: "Incontrai Fabregas, ma ero a Londra per un giocatore. Chivu sapeva, era già dell'Inter"

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Piero Ausilio si è reso disponibile per intervenire al Social Football Summit e tutti gli hanno chiesto la verità sul famoso incontro avuto con Cesc Fabregas in Inghilterra, dopo il quale è sembrato che l'Inter abbia virato su Chivu: "Cristian sapeva che era l’allenatore che volevamo e sapeva anche che c’era questo incontro. È poco mediatico, ma io ero a Londra per un altro motivo, per un giocatore".

E allora perché ha incontrato Fabregas? "Quando inizia la sua avventura al Como in Primavera, una delle prime cose che chiede è di venire a visitare Appiano Gentile, conoscere Inzaghi e i dirigenti, per capire i segreti del successo dell’Inter. Da lì è nato un rapporto, che poi anche negli anni di Inzaghi è continuato con pranzi o caffè. L’ho sempre trovato una persona molto curiosa, a Londra non è successo niente di diverso: Chivu sapeva di essere stato scelto come allenatore”.

Final Fantasy 14 patches "certain words" out of Keyboard Brawler, making it a bit less impossible

Final Fantasy 14 fans have spent weeks grappling with its new typing minigame, Keyboard Brawler, and the bots and impossible words it's been throwing at them – but Patch 7.58 has arrived, and it makes the Gold Saucer event a tad easier, thankfully.

Keyboard Brawler is great, if you ask me, even if it can be challenging. Its limited-time nature means you'll want to grind it out as soon as you're able, however, before it departs in January and the new expansion Evercold takes hold of your attention. The thing is, though, it's not so simple to tackle should a foreign word come up that doesn't match your keyboard's region – like, say, doppelgänger… and, until now, such words have indeed come up.

I've run into the dreaded umlaut-ridden words myself, panicking on the spot only to find out I could've used a keyboard shortcut or something similar to type them out. Better yet, Square Enix is on the case now itself with some tweaks as part of its official Patch 7.58 notes – yes, the very same Patch 7.58 that sees Europe and North America's servers merge in Party Finder (raiders, this one's truly for us) – addressing this and Keyboard Brawler's other problems.

For instance, "An issue when participating in Keybound Brawler wherein players were erroneously determined to be inactive and subsequently removed from the duty." And then, "An issue when participating in Keybound Brawler wherein, after disconnecting and reconnecting under certain conditions, words no longer appeared during the duty, and words would no longer refresh when re-entering the duty."

Square Enix also covers "an issue wherein setting the display of enhancements and enfeeblements to 'Display as Single Element' would result in status icons displaying in the wrong position after leaving Keybound Brawler – and, finally, "an issue when participating in Keybound Brawler wherein certain words not intended for the duty would appear." That's the one… the one I know I've been eagerly awaiting.

Patch 7.58 boasts quite a few more welcome adjustments to the MMO overall, of course, aside from Keyboard Brawler-specific changes, so I'd recommend giving it a full read. I know I'm excited to farm UMAD properly finally, personally.

Final Fantasy 14 Evercold won't suffer from Dawntrail's pacing issues, Yoshi-P says, as the MMO commits to respecting your time
