Man United Confidential: Jim Ratcliffe makes new 'brutal' cutbacks to ex-players' charity, Nathan Collins and Calvin Bassey among January targets, Luke Shaw eyes Anfield return and why club confiscated two anti-owner banners

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By CHRIS WHEELER, NORTHERN SPORTS WRITER and NATHAN SALT - FOOTBALL REPORTER

Published: 12:15 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 16:44 EST, 10 November 2026

Sir Jim Ratcliffe is known for being ruthless – as Michael Carrick may be about to find out – and it appears as though the Association of Former Manchester United Players has once again paid the price.

Two years ago, Ratcliffe made front-page news at Christmas after it emerged he was cutting a yearly £40,000 donation to the AFMUP charity, which has been helping ex-United players since 1985.

Last week organisers were informed at the last minute that the second of its two annual dinners couldn’t be staged as usual in the Manchester Suite at Old Trafford and would have to move elsewhere.

So while plans were being made for Sir Alex Ferguson’s glittering 40th anniversary bash in the Heritage Suite on Friday night, the AFMUP had to rely on Hotel Football – part-owned by Gary Neville and the Class of ’92 – coming to the rescue to host their event over the road 24 hours earlier. Ferguson, it has to be said, has been a big supporter of the charity over the years.

There are now concerns that AFMUP will never be allowed back to Old Trafford with United understood to be insisting on a total of 400 guests, which is double what the charity can provide.

Previously, the AFMUP gave half of the £40,000 donation back to the club to hire the suite in April and September, raising additional funds through ticket sales and auctions.

Sir Jim Ratcliffe (left) is known for being ruthless and it appears as though the Association of Former Manchester United Players has once again paid the price

When Neville challenged Ratcliffe over the ‘cruel decision’ to cut the donation in a one-on-one interview in March last year and said the club should consider organising the dinner itself and asking current players to attend, the minority owner replied: ‘Well nobody suggested that, I would have supported that.’

As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada joined Ferguson and former CEO David Gill at the dinner the following month – when Neville, Andy Cole and Jonny Evans were also present – and donated a VIP package for the auction worth £20,000.

Director of football Jason Wilcox then appeared at the next event in September and gave a rare interview on stage. In April of this year, Bryan Robson and Steve Bruce formed a popular double act as the main attraction.

Thursday’s dinner, which had already been moved from September 24 due to a double booking, was a celebration of the life of Duncan Edwards, who would have been 90 on October 1. Former chairman Martin Edwards and Gill were both present along with former goalkeeper Alex Stepney.

The AFMUP’s official filmmaker and publicist John Gubba highlighted the latest setback on his Man Utd The Religion YouTube channel, saying: ‘While the red carpet is being rolled out for Fergie, the club have been brutally abandoning the former players’ association.

‘The latest cutbacks by Sir Jim Ratcliffe have resulted in another kick in the teeth for the AFMUP. Staging their big event at the Theatre of Dreams was always a status that made the former players and their guests feel part of the football club. Being turfed out at short notice leaves a bitter taste.’ You can watch the full video here.

United sources say the club helped to facilitate an alternative venue at Hotel Football due to unavailability in the stadium, and insist that the charity will be welcome back to Old Trafford in future.

Berrada was present at Ferguson’s celebration dinner on Friday in which the United chief executive spoke about the importance of building a culture at Old Trafford.

He was one of 100 invited guests for the event to mark 40 years since Ferguson was appointed as United manager, moving from Aberdeen to replace Ron Atkinson in November 1986.

A number of Ferguson’s old players were present and posed for pictures with him including Michael Carrick, Brian McClair, Edwin van der Sar, Robin van Persie, David May, Wes Brown, Paul Parker, Dion Dublin, Denis Irwin, Jesper Blomqvist, Ryan Giggs, Gary Pallister, Javier Hernandez, Clayton Blackmore, Jonny Evans, John O’Shea, Darren Fletcher, Ronny Johnsen, Lee Martin, Nemanja Vidic and Quinton Fortune.

Ferguson’s former No2 Mike Phelan was also there along with Gill and kit man Albert Morgan.

There were 100 invited guests for the event to mark 40 years since Sir Alex Ferguson (third right) was appointed as United manager in November 1986

Luke Shaw is targeting a return to action away at Liverpool a week on Sunday, Confidential understands.

The 31-year-old has not featured since October 10 when he played 90 minutes against Tottenham, missing seven games through to the current international break.

That absence badly exposed United's lack of depth at left back, an area the hierarchy failed to address in the transfer window despite a clear need. Diogo Dalot, who was targeted by fans when he came off against Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday, has played poorly while filling in on his unfavoured side.

With Patrick Dorgu now back in the mix, too, Shaw is hoping to be in contention for the trip to Anfield in what would be a timely boost for a defence that has conceded the joint-most goals in all competitions of any team in Europe’s top five leagues this season.

Nothing has undermined Carrick's time as United head coach more than the defensive problems that have seen his team concede 15 goals in their last five games.

United chose to delay the overhaul of their defence until next year when they focused on rebuilding the midfield last summer, but it has proved to be a costly misjudgment with Matthijs de Ligt only just returning to action after a year out and a spate of injuries to Shaw, Lisandro Martinez, Dorgu and Noussair Mazraoui.

It may force United into the transfer market in January and Confidential understands Brentford centre back Nathan Collins and Fulham’s Calvin Bassey have joined Jarrad Branthwaite of Everton on the list of potential targets.

United also want a striker after failing to offload Joshua Zirkzee and sign cover for Benjamin Sesko in the summer, but a loan deal is more likely.

Brentford's Nathan Collins and Fulham's Nigeria star Calvin Bassey have both joined United's list of centre-back targets

As if the prospect of relegation isn’t nightmarish enough for United after they dropped to 18th place in the table, the cost of going down would be astronomical.

Although parachute payments from the Premier League would cushion the fall in the unlikely event that United are relegated, kit sponsors adidas have the option to scrap their record £900million, 10-year deal which was announced in 2023 but only came into effect last year.

Under the terms of the contract, the German sportswear giant can cancel it if they give the club one year’s notice. Adidas can also halve their annual payment to £45m if United aren’t playing in the Premier League, and hit the club with a £10m annual penalty if they are not in the Champions League.

Two banners protesting against United’s owners were confiscated by stewards during Sunday’s 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford.

One that read ‘Jim Can’t Fix This’ was seized because its connection to Jimmy Savile was deemed to be inappropriate. Another declaring ‘We Don’t Want A New Stadium, We Want New Owners’ was taken down because it was blocking the view of some fans.

Club insiders say protests will always be allowed as long as they remain within the regulations. United kept a close eye on Sunday’s demonstration with a more visible police presence in the Stretford End and stewards asking fans as they entered that section of the stadium which group they were with.

The protest, which once again involved green and gold balloons being released before kick-off, was jointly organised by The Red Army, The 1958, Manchester United Youth Supporters Club, South Stand United and Retro Reds.

However, it seemed like a strange decision to confiscate the banners considering that United were in possession of them for more than 30 hours before kick-off since 10am on Saturday morning.

They are stored in the same place in the Stretford End before every game, so it was odd for United to demand that they be taken down when they could have refused for them to be handed out in the first place.

A banner protesting against United's owners was confiscated during the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa

While goalkeeper Senne Lammens was giving his worst performance in a United shirt against Villa on Sunday, the man he replaced at Old Trafford was faring a little better in Turkey.

Andre Onana kept a clean sheet in Trabzonspor’s win over Alanyaspor as they moved into second place in the Super Lig table behind Galatasaray.

It came three days after a Europa League defeat by Freiburg when Onana couldn’t resist a little light ribbing of English reporters at the game.

Asked by journalists from Daily Mail Sport and the BBC for a quick word, the Cameroon international didn’t disappoint. ‘I don’t speak English, bro,’ Onana said, in perfect English. Classic.

→ Baca artikel asli

49ers insider says Raiders owner is main holdup to Maxx Crosby blockbuster trade

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

With the NFL trade deadline just hours away, one San Francisco 49ers insider said he still believes the 49ers' top target is still Maxx Crosby - and revealed what the holdup for a deal likely is.

San Francisco Standard columnist Tim Kawakami has been adamant for months that the 49ers would be most interested in trading for the star defensive end from the Las Vegas Raiders. He first wrote about the possibility after the 49ers were knocked out of last season's playoffs and even made a bold prediction of an offseason trade for Crosby all the way back in February.

The Raiders were clearly willing to move Crosby and agreed to trade him to the Baltimore Ravens in exchange for two first-round picks. But the Ravens ultimately killed the deal when the Ravens said Crosby failed his physical. Other teams reportedly expressed interest in a trade after that move, with ESPN reporting the Rams led the pack, but Crosby didn't want a trade in the offseason. Things could be different now, though. The Raiders started the season 3-0 but have fallen to 4-5 since and, while clearly improved, don't look like a Super Bowl contender.

That's where the 49ers fit in. They were among the teams interested in a Crosby trade after the Baltimore deal fell apart, and Kawakami continues to say the interest from the 49ers remains very high in a deal. On the "49ers Plus Minus" podcast he co-hosts with the Athletic reporter Matt Barrows, Kawakami discussed the 49ers' trade plans at length.

"If [the 49ers] are going to trade a first- or second-round pick, it's going to be for a defensive end - I don't think it's going to be receiver, I definitely don't think it's going to be for a cornerback," Kawakami said. "... I think, if you're going to go get a starter who's going to impact games, you know, right away, it's going to be a defensive end."

Barrows pointed out the struggles the 49ers have had to rush the opposing quarterbacks - even star Nick Bosa doesn't have a sack in the three games he's been healthy for so far this year. He also noticed how many 49ers players and staffers showed Crosby love after the game and noted the connection between Crosby and 49ers defensive line coach Kris Kocurek.

But Kawakami, the insider who has been pushing the "Crosby to the 49ers" trade harder than anyone, is now saying he doesn't expect a trade to happen in time for this season's Super Bowl run. His change of tune even caught Barrows off-guard, leading Barrows to directly ask why Kawakami backed off on a deal.

"I don't think [Raiders owner] Mark Davis is trading Maxx Crosby to the 49ers," Kawakami said. "Most of what I've been saying is that's who the 49ers want. I am not coming off of that. My second [point] has been that I think Crosby would certainly be happy with a trade to the 49ers. I don't think there's anything denying that, and we saw it on the field."

Kawakami said those points and the fact that the Raiders already made one trade agreement to deal Crosby are why he has been so adamant about the possibility. He even said the 49ers would be willing to part with a top draft pick to land Crosby. But in his estimation, the Raiders owner (and perhaps the longstanding rivalry between the former Bay Area rivals) is the biggest reason why any deal isn't happening.

"This [49ers] locker room really has circled Maxx Crosby as someone they think would fit," Kawakami said. "The same way they did Christian McCaffrey, the same way they did getting [DeForest] Buckner back - obviously that's a no-brainer - but they do that sometimes. Like, 'This is a guy that would really fit,' and it's Maxx Crosby.

"That's why I'm always thinking about him, but that does not mean that Mark Davis has to agree with it. And I don't think Mark Davis is trading Crosby to the 49ers."

The NFL trade deadline is 1 p.m. Pacific on Tuesday.

I'll be the first to admit I've said some incredibly questionable-sounding things while playing videogames. Or talking about them in public. But I would like to think that the one place where I would be extra careful about the words coming out of my mouth are in an airport, or on an airplane. Really, anywhere with an insane amount of security and an ever-pervasive fear of anything explosives-related.

Unfortunately, for passengers on board a Frontier Airlines flight earlier this week, however, one such gamer was not so cautious. As reported by CBS News, one plane at California's Ontario International Airport had to be evacuated over a bomb scare, only for it to be some guy playing Call of Duty with his pal.

According to reports, one passenger overheard words such as "bomb" and "explosion" and, rightly so, went and told staff about it. The situation led to both the plane and the east wing of Terminal 2 being evacuated, and a check of the aircraft which revealed that no explosives or bombs were on board.

After detaining and speaking to the culprit, it was revealed that he was, in fact, on FaceTime with his buddy while playing Call of Duty together. He was released without any charges, and the flight was reboarded and departed later that night.

I do feel a little bad for laughing about it—as a nervous flyer, I know for a fact that if I had been that passenger overhearing the conversation I may very well have gone into full-blown panic mode. I don't even like when someone doesn't put their phone on airplane mode as we're taking off!

But I'm also the type of person to watch my friend's Discord streams while I'm out and about, and I honestly dread to think about the kind of things passerbys have heard me say while spectating games. Hell, I'm sure even my neighbours have heard some absurd sentences leave my mouth when I've been raiding in Final Fantasy 14 with the windows wide open. If you're reading this neighbours, I'm sorry.

She can often be found causing mischief in Final Fantasy 14, using those experiences to write neat things about her favourite MMO. When she's not staring at her bunny girl she can be found sweating out rhythm games, pretending to be good at fighting games or spending far too much money at her local arcade.

Pochettino, Howe, Emery... or Carrick? Man Utd's managerial options

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With one victory in their first 10 Premier League matches, Manchester United have made their worst start to a top-flight campaign since the 1986-87 season.

They are 18th in the table, one point above bottom club Crystal Palace.

United directors will meet in Monaco on Wednesday to discuss Michael Carrick's future as his 10-month spell as head coach comes under major scrutiny.

If the 45-year-old former player departs, it would mean an eighth permanent boss for the Red Devils since Sir Alex Ferguson retired 13 years ago.

That would be as many managerial changes as they have made across the previous 68 years dating back to Sir Matt Busby's appointment in 1945.

BBC Sport takes a look at the leading candidates who could replace Carrick.

Mauricio Pochettino penned a new deal with with the USA in the summer [Getty Images]

Major trophies won: 1 Ligue 1, 1 French Cup

Premier League record: P294, W150; Win percentage: 51; Points per game ratio (PPG): 1.77

Did you know: Pochettino averaged 1.89 points per game during his spell at Tottenham, the best ratio of any Spurs manager to have taken charge of 25 or more Premier League games.

United's long-standing interest in the Argentine, 54, dates back a decade.

At Spurs, he moulded their best team in a generation, but ultimately came up short of winning a trophy, losing both the Champions League and League Cup finals, and posted second and third-placed finishes. Silverware did come at Paris St-Germain with a league title, but a spell at Chelsea proved tougher with a League Cup final defeat by Liverpool in 2024.

Having led the USA to the last 16 of the World Cup on home soil he signed a new contract taking him up to the 2030 tournament. Pochettino has an exciting group of young players to work with for the national team - led by Manchester City-bound 17-year-old Cavan Sullivan - but he has always made it clear he has unfinished business in the Premier League.

A reported buyout clause in Pochettino's contract is worth $10million (£7.44m), while it is also understood to contain clauses that would allow him to leave for specific high-profile jobs.

Carrick's Man Utd future to be decided at Wednesday meeting

What Howe could bring to Man Utd - and what are the challenges?

Julian Nagelsmann resigned in the summer after Germany lost to Paraguay on penalties in the World Cup [Getty Images]

Did you know: Only Hansi Flick (88.9%) has a better win percentage by a Bayern Munich manager in the Champions League than Nagelsmann's 83.3%.

Nagelsmann first impressed with his work at Hoffenheim, where he became the youngest head coach in Bundesliga history aged 28, leading them from relegation struggles to the Champions League.

He followed that by taking RB Leipzig to a Champions League semi-final in 2020 and Bayern Munich understandably came calling.

But despite winning the Bundesliga title in his first season he reportedly fell out with key players and did not survive a full second season.

In 2023, Nagelsmann took charge of a Germany side in transition, and they were narrowly beaten in the quarter-finals of Euro 2024 by eventual winners Spain, but stepped down in the summer after Die Mannschaft were humbled by Paraguay in the World Cup last 32.

Still only 39, his reputation may have taken a bit of a hit, but the German is still regarded as one of Europe's best coaches. Manchester United's director of recruitment Christopher Vivell also knows him well, having worked together at RB Leipzig.

Eddie Howe oversaw more Premier League wins (84) than any other Newcastle manager [BBC Sport]

Premier League record: P369 W140; Win percentage: 37.9; PPG: 1.36

Did you know: Eddie Howe (five) is one of only three managers with five or more wins against Mikel Arteta in all competitions, along with Pep Guardiola (11) and Jurgen Klopp (six).

His body of work in English football needs little introduction having led Bournemouth to the top flight for the first time in their history, before he confirmed his place in Newcastle folklore with their first major domestic trophy since lifting the FA Cup in 1955.

The League Cup final triumph over Liverpool was one of the greatest days in the club's history, while he also secured two top-five finishes to take the club back into the Champions League for the first time in 20 years.

Howe, 48, left in the summer after an underwhelming 12th-placed finish last term and Newcastle would only be due a a relatively small amount of compensation should he be tempted by the Old Trafford job.

Antonio Conte called Tottenham's players "selfish" when he left after 16 months in 2023 [Getty Images]

Major trophies won: 5 Serie A, 1 Premier League, 1 FA Cup

Premier League record: P132, W83; Win percentage: 62.9; PPG: 2.03

Did you know: Chelsea won 30 of their 38 Premier League matches as they claimed the title in Antonio Conte's first season in charge in 2016-17.

A serial winner, Conte's managerial credentials are not in doubt.

He left Napoli of his own accord this summer just 12 months after delivering only the fourth top-flight title in their history.

That added to Serie A triumphs at Juventus (three) and Inter Milan, and spells at Chelsea and Tottenham.

Conte, 57, would appear to be a no-brainer of a choice to take over at United, but could the Italian's fiery nature and history of falling out with clubs count against him?

His last stint in English football ended under a cloud of controversy as he left Tottenham in 2023 following criticism of the players and the club's culture.

While he had success at Chelsea, Conte also reportedly clashed with both players and the board.

Emery has also managed Arsenal and Paris St-Germain in his career [Getty Images]

Major trophies won: 5 Europa League, 1 Ligue 1, 2 French Cups, 2 French League Cups

Premier League record: P200, W101; Win percentage: 50.5; PPG: 1.75

Did you know: Unai Emery has the best Premier League win percentage (51%) and points per game ratio (1.75) of any permanent Aston Villa boss.

Vastly experienced, the Spaniard's stock is at an all-time high courtesy of the superb job he has done since taking over at Villa in October 2022.

Having taken the club into the European Cup/Champions League for the first time for 41 years, the 55-year-old then ended their 30-year wait for silverware with success in last season's Europa League.

Despite having lost the spine of his team over the summer, Emery's new-look side are finding their feet again and the chances of him leaving for United look even slimmer with recent news he is expected to sign a new contract.

Michael Carrick's Manchester United side have managed one win in 10 league games this season [Getty Images]

Premier League record: P29, W14; Win percentage: 48.3; PPG: 1.76

Did you know: After his initial appointment as United's interim boss in January, Michael Carrick had the most wins (12) and points (39) of any Premier League manager during the rest of the 2025-26 season.

Still clinging on to his job in the Old Trafford hot seat, could United's hierarchy actually decide to keep faith with Carrick?

After such a brilliant start and end to the season following his arrival in January, the wheels have come off spectacularly this term with only three wins from 15 matches in all competitions.

His team have at least shown fight with comebacks in recent games to beat Roma and salvage a point in a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa, but their overall stats are damning and the outlook doesn't look good for the 45-year-old, who won five Premier League titles and the Champions League as a United player.

The club are in the relegation zone and 18th is their lowest position after 10 games of a season for 40 years. It is also only the second time they have gone eight Premier League games without a victory.

8 Strongest Humans In The Lord Of The Rings, Ranked

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Lord of the Rings may be famous for its Elves and Orcs, but the human characters are legends in their own right. After the First Age, the Edain—the Men who fought against the Elves against Morgoth—were rewarded for their loyalty and sacrifice. The Valar raised the island of Númenór, and blessed the Númenóreans with longer lifespans, greater physical strength and endurance, and many other attributes.

Númenór is long-gone by the time of The Lord of the Rings (The Rings of Power is exploring its tragic fate). But survivors have scattered across Middle-earth, meaning the Valar's blessing has been absorbed (albeit diluted) into the wider race of Man. This blessing means many of the humans are incredible warriors, although none are as powerful as the Elves or as strong as Dwarves. Still, they're far more than ordinary mortals, as these eight examples prove.

Éomer is one of the Rohirrim, a people well-known for their horsemanship, physical toughness, and martial culture. What makes Éomer particularly impressive is that, unlike others on this list, he doesn't belong to a particularly strong Númenórean bloodline. He was only 28 during the War of the Ring, and yet proved himself an essential asset. His greatest advantage—one that few others here share—is that he excelled at cavalry combat.

Éomer is a fierce warrior who fought many different types of Orcs, including the monstrous Uruk-hai. After Théoden's​​​​​​​ death, he took command of the Rohirrim​​​​​​​, and led them to battle against Sauron's forces. In the novels, Éomer is driven into a ferocious battle-rage when he believes Éowyn and Merry have been killed as well.

Boromir is probably the physically strongest Man seen in the Third Age. A member of Gondor's ruling family, he's descended from the Númenóreans, and inherits much of their physical power. Tolkien deliberately describes him as a classic sword-and-sorcery hero, "broad shouldered," and demonstrates his prowess on many occasions. He fought on the front lines of many battles, helping defend Osgiliath.

Boromir did not survive. But his death shows just how impressive he was; he defended Merry and Pippin against an army of Uruk-hai, fighting against an overwhelming number of enemies. In the novels, his body was eventually found with many arrows in his body, his shield and sword both broken. But he was surrounded by the bodies of Uruk-hai, showing the Orcs paid a heavy price for attacking him.

Now let's head to Isildur, whose tale is being adapted in The Rings of Power. Isildur was a Númenórean, living during the Second Age, before Númenór's fall. As viewers are seeing, Isildur was noted for his heroism and fought on the front lines in many battles against Sauron and his forces. He played a key role in the Last Alliance, fighting alongside his father and the Elven High King Gil-galad. This led to his most famous achievement: Isildur was the one who cut the One Ring from Sauron's finger.

As great as Isildur's achievements may be, and as powerful a warrior as he was, others rank higher on this list for a simple reason; they had not just strength and skill, but also more hard-won experience. He had the misfortune of living at a time of crisis, but crisis became the norm on Middle-earth after Sauron's return. That's why he doesn't rank higher.

Aragorn is undeniably one of the greatest warriors of the Third Age. In part, that's because of his Númenórean bloodline; he's a direct descendant of the first King of Númenór, and the descendants of Elros retained unusually strong Númenóreans qualities. Viewers who are used to Viggo Mortenson's portrayal will be shocked to learn he's actually in his 70s by the time of the War of the Ring, but was still in his prime because of that genetic legacy.

Aragorn had spent decades fighting, traveling, and learning from some of the most skilled combatants in Middle-earth. He'd served as a military commander in Gondor, before operating independently as a Ranger who wandered the northern lands. This wide range of experience made Aragorn one of the most skilled, creative strategists as well as a formidable fighter in his own right. This experience means he ranks even higher than others who are probably physically stronger.

As formidable as the heroes of The Lord of the Rings may be, some of Tolkien's strongest human fighters are legends by the Third Age. Take, for example, Túrin Tarambar; a hero of the First Age who may well be the single most dangerous warrior. Hailing from the House of Hador, he was a formidable swordsman and a brave military commander who was feared by the Orcs and Morgoth's servants. He became famous for wielding Gurthang, a legendary black sword.

Túrin's greatest feat was the slaying of Glaurung, the first (and greatest) dragon. A mortal Man, he successfully killed one of the most powerful creatures ever created by Morgoth, before becoming celebrated as an Orc-slayer. Unfortunately, as impressive as Túrin may be, he also lacked judgment; his strategic mistakes ultimately helped Morgoth advance and conquer several lands.

Another hero of the First Age, Beren and his family became outlaws who undermined Morgoth due to their bravery and skill. Morgoth set such a hefty bounty on Beren's head that he was finally forced to leave his ancestral lands, and he passed through paths that even the Elves feared. before falling in love with Lúthien, princess of the Sindar. To win her hand in marriage, Beren carried out one of the most incredible feats in all Tolkien's stories.

The task was as simple as it was impossible: to break into Morgoth's stronghold, and steal one of the Silmarils from Morgoth's crown. At first, the attempt went wrong; Beren and his companion Finrod were captured by Sauron. But, with Lúthien's help, Beren achieved his task and successfully stole a Silmaril, even if doing so meant he lost a hand. He became the first to bind the races of Elves and Men.

Returning to the Third Age, Helm Hammerhand was probably the most physically intimidating warrior of this time. A King of Rohan, he became known for his tremendous strength, and for fighting his enemies with his bare hands. Unlike Aragorn, he did not have a Númenórean heritage; and that's particularly impressive, given the battles he fought in.

Helm's reign was plagued by conflict with the rival Dunlendings, They actually became afraid of him, believing he was more of a snow troll than a mortal Man, and his enemies came to believe he could not be killed by normal weapons. In the end, he died on one final sortie, frozen to death.

Another legend by the War of the Ring, Húrin Thalion is an absolute powerhouse. According to The Silmarillion, he was the greatest warrior of Men in the First Age. Not because of Númenórean bloodline, either; he lived before that was even a thing. Although he was unimpressive in stature, Húrin—whose epithet means "the Steadfast" or "the Strong"—proved himself by continuing to fight even when victory was impossible.

In the year FA 473, in the Nirnaeth Arnoediad, Húrin led a band of doomed warriors in a last, desperate attempt to delay the Orcs so allies could escape. Step by step, Morgoth's armies pushed them back to the banks of a river. There, Húrin fought on, until he was the only one left standing. Even then, he waded into the Orc army, shouting a wonderful cry of defiance: "Day will come again!"

Nebul Selects NetApp Novus to Scale Its Sovereign AI Cloud

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NetApp announced on November 10, 2026 that Nebul, a European AI-native cloud provider, is the first neocloud to adopt its Novus storage architecture for sovereign AI infrastructure. Nebul will use Novus in its sovereign Intelligent Cloud. The architecture is generally available and designed for up to 100 TB per second of aggregate throughput.

Nebul describes its Intelligent Cloud as a sovereign platform combining accelerated infrastructure, enterprise data services and production AI capabilities while preserving customer ownership and control. Beyond the adoption, the companies are exploring opportunities to accelerate sovereign-AI uptake by bringing together Nebul’s services and the NetApp Platform.

For Nebul’s multi-tenant environment, NetApp says Novus can scale performance, capacity and concurrency independently beneath one namespace as demand grows. Nebul founder and CEO Arnold Juffer said: “With this architecture, we can scale our AI Factory to serve growing customer demand without sacrificing the performance, security or compliance they depend on.”

Novus separates metadata, the information that maps file locations, from movement of the data itself. NetApp’s technical explanation describes a client requesting a layout from Novus Data Director and then communicating directly with ONTAP-based storage. Data Director is an independently scalable, software-defined control layer running on standard x86 compute, allowing the metadata and data planes to scale separately. This addresses the conflict that arises when small-file metadata operations and large checkpoint writes share controllers.

The Novus product documentation says the system works with standard in-kernel NFSv4.2 and pNFS Flex Files clients using nconnect, plus NVIDIA GPUDirect Storage. Operators therefore do not need custom client software across thousands of GPU servers. Additional ONTAP clusters add throughput without remounts or fragmentation, while every GPU continues to see the same namespace.

The ONTAP foundation also carries the data-governance side of the announcement. NetApp says the design is intended to let organizations using its platform extend existing data estates into sovereign cloud environments while preserving consistent security, protection and governance, including current jurisdiction and access policies. Willem Hendrickx, NetApp’s senior vice president and general manager for EMEA and LATAM, identified government, healthcare and biotech as regulated industries where providers must address compliance, security and multi-tenancy.

NetApp introduced Novus on September 29, 2026, making the architecture orderable that day. The initial release combines Data Director software on qualified Supermicro infrastructure with ONTAP data services on AFF A90 systems. NetApp described a path toward software-defined deployments over time while maintaining the same namespace, operating model and data foundation.

NetApp’s economic argument is that GPUs waiting on storage still depreciate without generating a return. At the September introduction, the company said utilization can fall below 30% under traditional architectures when storage cannot supply data quickly enough. The launch therefore framed storage throughput as a direct constraint on GPU-cluster economics.

NetApp technical fellow Arindam Banerjee’s explanation illustrates the bandwidth requirement with one GPU demanding as much as 2 GB per second and 50,000 GPUs requiring that rate simultaneously. It puts conventional storage arrays at 40–80 GB per second; reaching Novus’s aggregate target with such arrays would require more than 100 systems, each adding a namespace, failure domain and management overhead. The comparison connects the architecture’s single namespace to the operational overhead of scaling storage.

For Nebul, the adoption will test whether independently scaling metadata and data can support more tenants and GPUs without fragmenting data across storage systems, while jurisdiction, access and governance controls remain consistent. The operational challenge is to expand shared AI infrastructure without weakening the isolation and control that regulated customers require.

Maureen Kaiser steps down after 36 years with St. Henry volleyball

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Longtime St. Henry volleyball coach Maureen Kaiser is retiring from coaching after leading the Crusaders for 36 seasons, the school announced.

She is among the winningest coaches in state history, claiming 914 victories (889 at St. Henry). Under her watch, the Crusaders won eight All "A" state championships, most recently in 2024, claimed the 2021 state title and were state runners-up in 2005 and 2006. Her teams won 30 matches in a season eight times and claimed 25 district titles and nine Ninth Region titles.

"Coach Kaiser has built a program at St. Henry that represents faith, excellence, dedication and tradition. Her legacy goes far beyond wins and championships, as she has impacted generations of student-athletes and helped establish a standard of success that will continue for years to come," principal Grant Brannen said.

Kaiser is a member of the school's hall of fame, the Northern Kentucky Athletic Director's Hall of Fame and the Buddy LaRosa's High School Sports Hall of Fame. Hers is one of two names on the court in the Crusaders' gym alongside longtime boys basketball coach Dave Faust, who retired in 2025. She was a member of St. Henry's first state championship team in 1981.

"Coach Kaiser wasn't just one of the greatest coaches in Kentucky history — she was a builder of character, a mentor who shaped generations of student-athletes with integrity, passion, faith and heart. She built the St. Henry Volleyball program into a consistent state power year in and year out. We are so thankful for coach and what she has given to the SHDHS community. Her records and coaching accolades speak for themselves," athletic director Jim Demler said.

St. Henry is well-positioned to compete for the Ninth Region title in 2027. Only two seniors graduate in Kali McCane and Kayleigh McCormack. The returning cast is led by all-state honorees Violet Main and Kenadi Sieg.

Kaiser will continue to teach health at the school.

Charta Health Raises $55M Series B to Expand AI Chart Review

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Charta Health announced on November 10, 2026, that it had raised a $55 million Series B to expand its AI chart-review technology. Insight Partners led the financing, which also drew QuantumLight and returning investors Madrona, Bain Capital Ventures and South Park Commons.

Charta will put the capital toward further AI development for provider customers, entry into additional specialties and its goal of establishing one trusted interpretation of a chart before billing. Alongside the financing, it broadened Charta for Payers with a suite initially aimed at Medicare Advantage and Affordable Care Act Marketplace plans.

Charta says its provider software reviews every chart before billing against coding guidelines, payer rules and each customer’s clinical protocols. Findings appear for human approval inside the electronic health record, leaving staff to validate the issues flagged by the system.

The software applies large language models to unstructured material such as free-text notes, scanned orders, laboratory results and images. Its task is to establish what care was delivered and whether the documentation supports the billing, moving accuracy checks ahead of the denials and appeals that can follow a claim.

Compliance and medical leaders can also examine documentation deficiencies, error patterns and provider performance across patient encounters. American Family Care, Grow Therapy and Pediatric Associates Family of Companies are among the healthcare enterprises using the technology. Charta reports consistent double-digit reductions in denial rates for fee-for-service billing.

The payer products look for documented conditions that were not coded and for coded diagnoses that lack support in the chart. Their six product areas span retrospective diagnosis coding, automated second-pass coding, a smart coding workspace, prospective risk-adjustment intelligence, diagnosis validation and audit workflow, and quality care-gap detection. Charta says it designed the suite with one of the nation’s largest health insurers, without naming the partner.

The payer platform extracts structured clinical and encounter intelligence from records that plans already retrieve, allowing the same evidence to support retrospective coding, second-pass quality assurance, audit work and prospective workflows. Charta says its automated second-pass product applies AI to 100% of coded charts, while the smart coding workspace presents supporting evidence, citations, and additions and deletions for validation in one review.

For retrospective risk adjustment, AI presents clinical evidence to human coders, who use customer-specific guidelines to validate additional diagnoses and flag unsupported codes for deletion. Smart Scan pre-screens the chart population to identify records with enough potential value to warrant full review. Charta advertises 98% accuracy for diagnosis coding in this workflow, based on the combination of AI evidence recall and human validation.

Insight Partners co-founder and managing director Jeff Horing pointed to Charta’s pairing of AI and customer-specific coding expertise across providers and payers. “As payers and providers both adopt AI, we believe the opportunity lies in getting the underlying record, or the ‘ground truth,’ right before a claim is submitted,” he said.

The new round follows Charta’s $22 million Series A, announced on July 22, 2025 and led by Bain Capital Ventures, with Madrona, SV Angel, Refract Ventures and South Park Commons participating. Charta said its total funding exceeded $30 million at that time. Where the earlier announcement focused on automating provider chart review, this one pairs continued provider development with a broader payer product rollout.

The expansion makes a shared chart record the central test of Charta’s model. The same evidence can help a provider submit a better-supported claim and help a payer identify an unsupported diagnosis; the commercial challenge is making that review dependable enough for both sides while humans remain responsible for the coding decisions.

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic is targeting a valuation of up to $1.02 trillion, based on its proposed IPO price range of $70–$80 per share.

The company could raise $69.4 billion through its public offering and another $26 billion through a private placement.

Nvidia and Alphabet are each committing $10 billion, highlighting the financial exposure of major technology companies to Anthropic's growth.

Anthropic’s planned IPO is emerging as a major test for the AI industry, with the Claude developer targeting a valuation of approximately $1 trillion. Backed by Nvidia and other technology companies, the offering could raise more than $90 billion through public and private share sales. Its market debut comes as investors increasingly scrutinise the cost of developing artificial intelligence and the returns on infrastructure spending. Can Anthropic justify its ambitious valuation, and what could its listing mean for Nvidia and other AI stocks?

Anthropic has proposed selling 865 million shares at $70–$80 apiece, potentially raising up to $69.4 billion. Based on outstanding shares, the upper end of the range implies a valuation of approximately $1.02 trillion. Including options and other potential shares, the fully diluted valuation could reach around $1.4 trillion.

Anthropic is seeking to raise up to $69.3B in its IPO, below earlier expectations of $80B-$100B, Reuters reports.It plans to sell 866.7M new shares at $70-$80 each, with all proceeds going to the company.Trading could begin next week on Nasdaq under $ANT. pic.twitter.com/HoF0ENA2T6

The company is also arranging a separate $26 billion private placement involving Nvidia, Alphabet, Microsoft, AMD, Broadcom, Micron and TSMC. Google and Nvidia are each committing approximately $10 billion, making them the largest participants in the arrangement.

Anthropic plans to list on Nasdaq under the ticker symbol ANT. The offering’s final price and trading date remain subject to the IPO process.

Nvidia’s $10 billion commitment to Anthropic highlights its growing financial exposure to the AI ecosystem. The company already benefits from demand for GPUs used to train and operate advanced AI models. Anthropic’s expansion could support further demand for computing capacity, particularly as enterprise adoption of Claude increases.

However, the investment also raises questions about interconnected financing in the AI industry. Nvidia is investing in a company that needs substantial computing infrastructure, including its own products. Investors will therefore examine whether AI infrastructure spending is generating sustainable customer demand and revenue rather than depending heavily on capital flowing between industry participants.

Anthropic’s growth has been accompanied by substantial infrastructure commitments. Its latest filing disclosed approximately $17 billion in third-quarter revenue and $33.2 billion in revenue for the first nine months of 2026. However, the company also reported a nine-month net loss of $104 billion and computing and infrastructure commitments of approximately $900.9 billion.

The figures highlight the financial challenge facing advanced AI developers. Revenue growth remains important, but investors will increasingly assess operating costs, capital requirements and the path towards profitability.

Anthropic’s public listing could provide an important indication of how much investors are willing to pay for large-scale AI businesses. Strong demand could support confidence across the technology sector, while a weaker reception could renew concerns about elevated AI valuations.

For Nvidia and its technology partners, the central question is whether the enormous investment in AI infrastructure can translate into sustainable returns. Anthropic’s IPO will put that question directly before public-market investors.

Can you use WD-40 on your electronics?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The short answer: no. The long answer: there's a different WD-40 product you can use.

It can quiet your hinges, get rid of rust and even help break apart stuck LEGO bricks — WD-40 is the magic spray that can do just about anything. It has been around since 1953, originally developed for use in the aerospace industry (specifically, protecting the exterior of the Atlas Missile from rust and corrosion), and has become a staple in just about every home. However, when it comes to your electronics, WD-40 is one of the worst possible cleaners you could reach for.

Though the "WD" in the product's name stands for "water displacement," as a whole it contains a mix of petroleum-based lubricants and anti-corrosion ingredients alongside those designed to displace water; this is why it excels both at lubrication and cleaning. From its inception in the '50s, the company has grown exponentially, expanding both the ways customers can buy regular WD-40, like various bottle sizes and applicators, as well as adding new products to the line-up like other specialized lubricants and degreasers. Nowadays, there are some products the company makes that are more suitable for your electronics, but the original formula should stay far away from them.

There are multiple reasons why WD-40 and sensitive electronic parts don't mix. For one, it can be completely counterproductive when it comes to cleaning, as its unique chemical makeup leaves a residue behind that can actually attract more dust and dirt over time. This residue is great when used for something like greasing a hinge, but much less so on your devices. It might look clean at first, but that area is now going to be a grime magnet and just worsen the problem you were attempting to solve.

Even worse is that the residue left behind is both electrically conductive and flammable. This means that your device is essentially now a ticking bomb, not only for future grime but actual hazardous reactions, particularly if you've used it to do something like clean a USB port and it has reached key internal parts. Eventually, the residue's conductivity could cause a component to short out, or in extreme cases cause an electrical fire. As a whole, using WD-40 on your electronics is all drawbacks and no benefits.

If you're steadfastly dedicated to the WD-40 brand, they do offer an alternative you can safely utilize with your electronics. WD-40 Specialist Contact Cleaner is designed to leave no residue, be nonconductive and dry quickly, eliminating all the pitfalls associated with the original WD-40. It's not the only contact cleaner on the market, but for those who trust the brand and enjoy their straw-based applicator design, it's a solid choice.

However, a specialized product isn't necessarily a must-have purchase when it comes to keeping your electronics clean. An arsenal consisting solely of high-proof (90-99%) isopropyl alcohol for use on dirty components and a can of compressed air, which is great for things like cleaning laptop fans and keyboards, is generally enough to satisfy most needs, especially when paired with a good-quality microfiber cloth. For a deeper look at the best way to clean your devices, check out how to properly sanitize your tech.

→ Baca artikel asli

DISNEY THEATRICAL GROUP ANNOUNCES 200 ELEMENTARY SCHOOLS SELECTED FOR 2026-27 DISNEY MUSICALS IN SCHOOLS: STAGE CONNECT PROGRAM

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

THE FREE ONLINE TEACHER TRAINING AND PERFORMANCE PROGRAM WILL HELP SCHOOLS IN 43 STATES AND TERRITORIES PRODUCE THEIR FIRST SCHOOL MUSICAL

Applications Now Open for the 2027-28 School Year

NEW YORK, Nov. 10, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Today, Disney Theatrical Group announced the 200 public elementary schools to participate in the Disney Musicals in Schools: Stage Connect program for the 2026-27 school year. Stage Connect, an expansion of Disney Musicals in Schools, is a program that delivers high-quality teacher training through online learning, bringing the magic of musical theatre to schools that do not have a Disney Musicals in Schools residency program in their area. This year's Stage Connect cohort was selected from more than 800 applications and will reach over 10,000 students, nearly three times the size of the 70-school inaugural class that launched the program in fall 2025.

The selected schools are in 43 states and U.S. territories, spanning communities from coast to coast and across the Pacific and Caribbean. 2026-27 recipients include schools in rural communities like Emerson, Arkansas, cities like Milwaukee, Wisconsin, communities in American Samoa, Hawaii, and Puerto Rico, as well as a school on a U.S. military base in Sicily, Italy. Many of these communities are far from any in-person Disney Musicals in Schools program, and Stage Connect brings the opportunity directly to them.

Each selected school will receive access to the Stage Connect online teacher training platform, performance rights to a Disney KIDS show, and a ShowKit of materials, including scripts, director's guides, and rehearsal tracks, all at no cost. Schools will choose from 101 Dalmatians, Aladdin, The Aristocats, Finding Nemo, Frozen, The Jungle Book, The Lion King, The Little Mermaid, and Winnie the Pooh. Before beginning rehearsals, educators will complete the Stage Connect courses so they can lead their school's first musical regardless of prior theatre experience. Participating schools will also be matched with a Disney Production Mentor who provides virtual coaching to teachers throughout the rehearsal and production experience.

"These 200 schools are in places as far apart as American Samoa and a military base in Sicily. That's nearly 11,000 miles," said Lisa Mitchell, Director of Education & Audience Engagement at Disney Theatrical Group. "What they have in common is a school community ready to make theatre. Stage Connect bridges that distance, so a teacher's location no longer decides whether they have the training and resources for their students to perform a musical and enjoy the many benefits of arts education."

To date, the combined impact of both delivery models of Disney Musicals in Schools has launched theatre programs in over 1,000 schools, representing over 150,000 student participants.

Applications for the 2027-28 school year are now open and are due Monday, March 1, 2027. Any public or charter elementary school in the United States and its territories is eligible to apply. For more information and to apply, visit DisneyMusicalsInSchools.com.

About Disney Musicals in Schools: Disney Musicals in Schools is a Disney Theatrical Group initiative that ignites a passion for storytelling by building sustainable theatre programs in public elementary schools. Since launching in 2009, the program has partnered with local performing arts organizations in 24 cities across the U.S. and U.K. and, through Stage Connect, now reaches schools nationwide. For more information, visit DisneyMusicalsInSchools.com.

Disney Musicals in Schools is one of Disney's signature programs supporting Future Generations to become their best selves â the world's future storytellers, dreamers, heroes and leaders. Learn more at Disney.com/FutureGenerations.

Prices Prop Up Travel as Demand Cools

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Photo Credit: A pedestrian walking alongside the Tiber River in Rome. Pexels / Bruna Santos

Travel interest is slipping, but revenue is holding because prices are doing the work. That cushion only lasts as long as travelers keep paying more, which makes falling search activity worth watching closely heading into 2027.

Across global markets, fewer travelers are searching for trips and fewer say they plan to travel, but the slowdown hasn’t reached the bottom line yet. Higher airfares and accommodation rates are holding up revenue as traveler interest cools.

The Skift Travel Health Index stood at 99 in September, down 1% year-on-year. The destinations index, which measures travel interest through online search activity, fell by 12% to 88, as inbound flight and hotel searches weakened across most tracked markets.

Decision intelligence for travel leaders.

Travel demand signals softened in September 2026 while pricing kept revenue intact. The Skift Travel Health Index stood at 99, down 1% year-on-year, and the destinations index, which tracks travel interest through online search activity, fell 12% to 88 as inbound flight and hotel searches weakened across most tracked markets. Inbound flight search growth was negative in the majority of the 21 destinations tracked, with the United Arab Emirates down 37%, Hong Kong down 18%, Australia down 16%, and Mexico down 15%, while China (+11%), India (+7%), and Italy (+5%) gained. Intent data points the same way: the European Travel Commission found the share of global travelers planning a long-haul trip fell from 55% in autumn 2025 to 50% in autumn 2026, and interest in visiting Europe slipped from 38% to 34%. The share of travelers who had already booked flights stayed broadly unchanged, so the cooling is concentrated at the top of the funnel. Higher airfares and accommodation rates are holding up revenue as traveler interest cools.

NYSPHSAA schedule for Section 1 boys soccer teams in NYS tournament

Section 1 has strengthened its claim as a soccer hotbed with another remarkable season in which three local teams are vying this weekend for state championships. Five Lower Hudson Valley teams advanced to their respective regional finals, but it was Mamaroneck, Briarcliff and North Salem that emerged, advancing to the state semifinals.

North Salem is after a third straight Class C state title. The Tigers graduated several core pieces from that powerhouse squad, and they'll be an underdog in the final four, but it would be foolhardy to underestimate them. Mamaroneck has surged throughout the season, cutting its teeth against a difficult schedule and earning its way into the discussions of championship contenders. They're ranked third among Class AAA teams, according to the New York State Sportswriters Association, but already toppled the No. 2 and No. 4 teams in the regional rounds.

Briarcliff is competing in the state tournament for the first time since 2018, but this talented and confident group has taken off in the postseason. The Bears outscoring their regional opponents by a combined 9-1 is an indication of how legitimate their title hopes are.

I really wasn't expecting Microsoft Paint to turn up in the cybersecurity headlines, but here we are. MS disclosed and patched a security vulnerability within the software on October 13, but it's worth checking your own Paint version to see if you've received the update.

Some users don't have automatic Microsoft Store updates enabled, especially those on work machines. As Paint is updated via the store, you may need to manually update to version 11.2609.401.0 to keep your machine secure.

To confuse things further, International Cyber Digest reports that the MS store was providing older versions to some users until recently, and Windows Latest didn't seem to receive the new version even with auto updates enabled. Or, there's been some mixup with release numbers. It's genuinely difficult to tell at this point, with differing information coming from multiple sources.

The vulnerability was initially given an 8.1 severity rating on the CVSS scale, but Microsoft downgraded it to a 7.0 score on November 9 according to Threat Defence. MS also changed its description of the bug to that of a local exploit (requiring a user to open a crafted file) rather than a network attack vector, .

Regardless, to check whether your Paint version is hopefully up-to-date (what a world we live in), open the app, go to the Settings menu, and check the version number beneath the "About Paint" section. If it's lower than version 11.2609.401.0, open the Microsoft Store and manually update the app. Or, if your Windows 11 settings don't allow user updates, contact your sysadmin.

You can also check your Paint version within Windows Powershell by running:

Honestly, this is the biggest omnishambles of conflicting cybersecurity info I've seen in a while. Some outlets initially reported the bug as a no-click security flaw, while others have suggested that the vulnerability could potentially be exploited via a specially crafted image. Microsoft shifting the security goalposts as it goes really hasn't helped, nor has its apparently messy roll out of the patched version.

Police Raid $4.4 Million San Francisco Squatter Mansion—Arresting Woman and Rescuing 7 Dogs

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The woman allegedly at the center of a squatting dispute in San Francisco’s luxury Sea Cliff community was arrested on Friday during a raid of the property and following a monthslong investigation into her claims on the home. Diana Peng, 42, was charged with fraud, driving without a license, narcotics possession, improper care of animals, and being a felon in possession of a firearm and ammunition. Police also removed seven dogs from the home and put them in the care of a local shelter.

Peng and her alleged accomplice, Brandon Aadee, are in the midst of a civil lawsuit brought by San Francisco City Attorney David Chiu. Chiu accused the pair of illegally obtaining the $4.4 million property through forged, fraudulently notarized deeds, intending to sell it.

According to Chiu’s complaint, Peng and Aadee took over the 3,000-square-foot Tudor-style home at 250 32nd Ave. in 2025 by claiming that the previous owner had transferred the deed to Aadee. Peng and Aadee transferred the property title back and forth between them eight times, and at least five of those were forged, including the initial deed from the legitimate owner, Wei Wang, which includes a forged notary signature.

Wang and her husband, Xiangdong Guo, purchased the property in 2013 for $2.41 million. But the pair moved abroad around 2019, and the property fell into disrepair and appeared to be abandoned.

It also racked up more than $340,000 in unpaid property taxes, which prevented a sale of the property.

This past October, however, the San Francisco Board of Supervisors approved a resolution to pay off the property taxes, paving the way for the home to be put up for auction later this year.

Neighbors in the wealthy enclave, where both Sharon Stone and Twitter founder Jack Dorsey have homes, said they spent much of the past year on edge, watching people come and go from the property at all hours.

"I'm probably in touch with one city official or another at least six times every day," neighbor Whitney Lynn told ABC 7. "We really have been asking for help, and it feels like people finally heard us.Most recently, in August, a neighbor called 311 to complain that the property was “being used as a group home.”

“The home was abandoned for over 5 years, and squatters moved in May 2025 using burglary tools. Over the past couple of days, a new group of about a dozen squatters moved in," the complaint read. Nearly a dozen complaints were also filed with the Department of Building Inspection, the most recent on Oct. 13. That complaint stated that unpermitted plumbing and irrigation work had been taking place in the backyard.

Neighbors told the San Francisco Chronicle that Friday’s raid lasted about four hours and that police removed several boxes and bags of documents. Police went in with guns drawn and came out with Peng, and seven dogs were removed from the property and taken to a local shelter. Police also towed Peng's Porsche.

Deb Campbell, the public information officer with San Francisco Animal Care and Control, tells Realtor.com® the two adult pit bulls and five puppies are “doing well in our care.” She otherwise couldn’t comment on the animals' condition because of the ongoing investigation.

“We appreciate SFPD’s work to pursue accountability and keep our neighborhoods safe,” Jen Kwart, the communications director for the city attorney’s office, tells Realtor.com. "As our civil case moves forward, we look forward to continuing to work closely with SFPD to ensure the courts understand that Defendants are effectively trying to steal a house and sell it to make millions in profit.”

Both Peng and Aadee have long criminal records. In 2008, she was convicted of perjury and sentenced to 90 days in jail and three years’ probation. Four years later, she pleaded guilty to identity theft and burglary and served seven months in prison. And then in 2015, she was sentenced to an additional two years in jail for forgery of a driver’s license and using another person's identification.

In 2009, Aadee was convicted of defrauding FEMA out of thousands of dollars and ordered to pay $48,000 in restitution. He also has convictions for credit card fraud and passing bad checks.

Following Friday’s raid, Peng was booked and is being held without bail at the San Francisco County Jail.

Total revenue was $149.2 million compared with $402.2 million a year ago. Crypto services revenue is presented gross, it moves with trading volume and is substantially offset by crypto costs and execution fees. Bakkt reported GAAP net income of $14.3 million or $0.31 per diluted share including a $26.8 million noncash gain on the Transchem warrants. On an operating basis, our operating loss was $14.4 million, 43% narrower than a year ago. We remain focused on cost discipline.

Operating expenses, excluding crypto costs and execution fees, were $16.1 million down 40% from $26.7 million a year ago and down 24% excluding last year's $5.6 million of restructuring and impairment charges. We ended the quarter with $37.3 million of cash and restricted cash. No long term debt, and no shares sold under our at the market program. That gives us the flexibility to keep investing in commercial activation while remaining disciplined on the cost side. Adjusted EBITDA was a loss of $11.8 million compared with a loss of $14.8 million a year ago. A $3 million improvement year over year As Daniel explained, transaction economics depend on product mix, corridors, and the balance of flows.

Those factors are still developing and are expected to evolve through much of next year. We are not providing a stabilized gross margin assumption today. Our priority is to convert growing client activity into a sustainable operating results. Back to Akshay for our execution priorities. Akshay?

Akshay Naheta: Our execution priorities for the fourth quarter and the first half of 27 are on this slide. And they follow directly from what you have heard today. Let me close with our 4 things to take away. First, the volume targets rest on the existing pipeline. Clients already in integration, converted by a commercial team now in their seats. Second, the ramp began in October. And builds in 2 ways: new clients going live, and live clients routing more of their settlement treasury and payments through the platform. Third, volume converts to revenue through transaction. And processing fees and foreign exchange conversion. And as flows across the network become more 2 way, we expect transaction margins to improve.

And finally, from December, the Bakkt Engine report will show you actual monthly volume so you can measure our progress against the targets we have set. We will judge our progress by client activity, revenue, and operating performance and ask you to do the same. Thank you for your time and your continued support. Operator, we are ready to take questions.

Operator: Thank you. I am showing no questions in the queue. And that concludes today's conference call. Thank you for joining us. You may now disconnect. You may now disconnect.

Panthers restructure Taylor Moton’s contract to free up cap room

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The NFL trade deadline is 4:00pm Eastern time today (Tuesday). There's been more activity around the trade deadline in recent years as teams have started to find the utility in becoming buyers or sellers based on the way their seasons are going. The Carolina Panthers are just 3-5, but they do technically lead the NFC South. That would suggest they're more likely to be buyers at the deadline if anything, and they did a bit of bookkeeping to confirm that.

According to Spotrac, the Panthers restructured offensive tackle Taylor Moton's deal to save themselves $3.5 million against this season's salary cap. It's the typical restructure move. They converted some of his base salary (which counts entirely against this season's cap) to a signing bonus (which is spread across the length of the deal). The team was right at the salary cap before this move, and now they have some wiggle room to add a piece at the deadline if there's one that suits them.

The $3.5 million number isn't going to fit any blockbuster acquisition, even if it is only a player's base salary. If anything, the Panthers will peruse the league's worst teams for some inexpensive veterans that can fill the void in the secondary while Jaycee Horn, Mike Jackson, and Will Lee recover from their injuries. Same could be said for the offensive line, where injuries have caused problems and subpar play has caused even more.

However, it's more likely that his move is to give the team some wiggle room to sign or claim free agents. The team hosted cornerback Jack Jones for a visit the other day but haven't yet offered him a contract. Moves like that would require the kind of space the Moton restructure opens up, so that seems like the route in which the Panthers will spend the extra money they've freed up.

Airline Interline, Codeshare, Alliance, And Joint Venture Agreements Explained

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Airline partnerships are more complex and advanced than ever before, both for better and worse. About 25 years ago the Star Alliance was formed, which was revolutionary at the time. SkyTeam and oneworld quickly joined the scene.

While they all still exist, the global alliances have definitely been deemphasized a bit in recent years. After all, with these alliances having so many members, how much can loosely affiliated airlines really have in common?

Airlines want to work with other airlines that complement them best, and this can come in many different forms. Just to give a few examples of partnerships outside of the traditional alliances:

Airlines have several different levels of cooperation, ranging from interline agreements, to codeshare agreements, to alliance membership, to joint venture agreements. In this post, I’d like to take a closer at the differences between these agreements. Before I do so, let me add two disclaimers:

With that out of the way, let’s take a look at these four kinds of agreements.

Interline agreements are the most basic kinds of agreements you can have between airlines. An interline agreement is simply a commercial agreement between airlines to handle passengers when they’re traveling on multiple airlines on the same itinerary, and it allows an airline to issue a ticket on another airline (though not necessarily on any sort of favorable commercial terms).

This allows passengers to check their bags through to their final destination, get boarding passes all way to their destination, potentially be rebooked on another airline in the event of irregular operations, etc.

This is a basic level of cooperation, so there are airlines that have interline agreements but don’t otherwise have robust partnerships. For example, Etihad doesn’t belong to a major global alliance, but has an interline agreement with dozens of airlines, ranging from American, to Emirates, to JetBlue, to United, to Qatar Airways.

As you can see, this is a level of cooperation we even see between airlines that don’t partner with one another, and that may not even be fond of one another.

It is worth noting that in some cases airlines specifically pull out of interline agreements. For example, many years ago Delta cut its interline agreement with American. Why? At least in part it’s because Delta found that American was rebooking more passengers on Delta during irregular operations than the other way around, so the carrier no longer thought the arrangement was in its best interest.

A codeshare agreement is the next level of cooperation between airlines. This is when two airlines realize there’s value in working together, and they decide they want to place their “codes” on one another’s flights. Typically the main benefit of this is that it drums up business for the airlines that are in a codeshare agreement with one another.

Just to give an example, Alaska Airlines and Icelandair have a codeshare agreement. Icelandair flies to certain gateways in the United States, and then Alaska Airlines provides some of the “feed” for those flights in the form of codeshares.

For example, if you want to fly from Reykjavik to Los Angeles, Icelandair doesn’t operate that route. However, Icelandair flies to Portland, and then you can book a Portland to Los Angeles flight on Alaska Airlines as an Icelandair codeshare.

When you book a codeshare flight, you’ll notice that the flight number is different. So if you booked through Icelandair, your Portland to Los Angeles flight would also have an Icelandair flight number (in this case FI7696). To be clear:

With these codeshare agreements, generally neither airline is getting a huge cut for booking someone on the other airline. Most of the ticket revenue goes to the operating carrier, and often a small amount goes to the airline that sold the flight. However, the biggest advantage of this arrangement is to boost business for both airlines by increasing the number of flights that passengers have access to.

The “big three” alliances — oneworld, SkyTeam, and Star Alliance — have only developed in the past 25 years, though in the meantime many major global carriers belong to one of them. The basic way that alliances work is as follows:

I’d note that different airlines place different levels of emphasis on these alliances, even if they choose to belong to them. For example, Delta is a founding member of SkyTeam, but the airline is heavily focused on joint ventures and airlines it owns a stake in, and cares very little about the airlines it simply shares an alliance with.

If a codeshare agreement is like dating, then a joint venture is like getting married. A joint venture agreement is a massive business decision that typically requires extensive government approval. When airlines form a joint venture they coordinate pricing and schedules, and have a revenue sharing agreement.

Exactly how the revenue sharing works depends on the specific arrangement, but the idea is that two airlines are essentially acting as one under a joint venture.

Note that typically airlines form joint ventures between specific regions, which is why this is different than an outright merger. For example, American has a transatlantic joint venture with British Airways, Finnair, Iberia, and Aer Lingus, while the airline has two transpacific joint ventures, with both Japan Airlines and Qantas (the two are separate arrangements).

From the perspective of a consumer, a joint venture is both good and bad. The good news is that typically it gives you the most flight options in terms of schedules, since the airlines are operating as one. Airlines also often try to make the experience as consistent across brands as possible. The downside is that it’s like a competitor being eliminated in the market, so it could lead to higher fares as it reduces competition.

There are so many different types of agreements in the airline industry nowadays. While the exact terms vary with every partnership, I think the easiest way to sum it up is that an interline agreement is like a friendship, a codeshare agreement is like an engagement, a joint venture is like a marriage, and an alliance is like having a big family, with everyone sort of doing their own thing.

There’s a lot more to all of this, but hopefully the above provides a pretty fair and basic summary. People often misuse these terms, so I figure it’s worth recapping every so often.

¡Vamos, España!

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With Spain fresh off a World Cup win, there’s no better time to visit Madrid and the cooler northern regions of Asturias and Galicia

I lived in Spain in the early aughts, when the country was experiencing a mic-drop moment. The shiny, futuristic Frank Gehry–designed Guggenheim Museum had gone up in Bilbao a few years earlier, setting off what is now known as the Bilbao effect: a phenomenon that describes how investing in a visionary architecture project can transform a place. Food-obsessed travelers were flocking to a small beach town in a remote corner of northern Spain just to experience a meal at El Bulli, the legendary, now closed, three-Michelin-star restaurant headed up by Ferran Adrià.

Spain is now in the midst of another glow-up, thanks in part to its World Cup win, and Madrid is front and center. A recent influx of expats from South and North America has locals calling the city the “Miami of Europe.” But don’t let that dissuade you. Mallorquín designer Rosa Esteva, who designs the elegant, made-in-Spain fashion brand Cortana, emphasizes “the charm of those glorious old places that have survived through time.” That means staying at hotels like the intimate Hotel Orfila, booking a table at La Parra, wandering through El Rastro, Madrid’s legendary flea market, and checking out a late-night flamenco show at Tablao de la Villa. At the same time, contemporary places like Esteva’s new shop in the Salamanca neighborhood, the modern tapas bar Sala de Despiece, and the contemporary-art gallery Albarrán Bourdais are worth checking out.

The typical Spanish itinerary often includes a few days in Barcelona to take in the Gaudí before heading south to the beaches of Andalusia. But Vella González, the founder of fashion brand Boxcalf, which has a flagship in Madrid’s Malasaña neighborhood, extols northern Spain’s charms and cooler climate. “For non-Spaniards, it’s an undiscovered paradise,” she says. Start in San Sebastián, one of the world’s most celebrated gastronomic capitals, and eat your way through its pintxos bars, finishing with a steak at the Basque grill house Portuetxe.

In Bilbao, discover the next generation of art spaces, such as the light-filled expansion of the Bilbao Fine Arts Museum, designed by Norman Foster and Luis María Uriarte. The Bilbao effect can be felt in nearby Santander, thanks to the new Faro Santander museum and cultural space, a historic building reimagined by David Chipperfield and currently showcasing the works of surrealist painter Leonora Carrington. Move on to the pristine beaches, green hills, and exceptional dining scene of the Asturias and Galicia regions. In Asturias, splurge on a stay at the remote wabi-sabi styled Solo Palacio and explore the old-growth forests and prehistoric rock shelters of Las Ubiñas-La Mesa Natural Park. Cozy up in the Casa Beatnik Country House hotel in Galicia, and walk the last 10 miles of the famed Camino de Santiago trail to Santiago de Compostela and its Old Town, a UNESCO World Heritage site. It’s an idyllic ending to a new kind of Spanish pilgrimage.
